#6919. Presenting: Mason Thames and Mckenna Grace’s Full Relationship Timeline

Favorite costars turned lovers? It might just be these two.

Fact of life: Everyone loves a good showmance, which is why the Regretting You press tour had the internet positively spiraling. For months, the film’s stars Mckenna Grace and Mason Thames teased fans about whether or not they were dating in real life with their flirty off-screen behavior…until, at long last, they officially hard-launched their relationship.

And by hard-launched, I mean Mckenna brought Mason as her date to the biggest night in Hollywood, the 2026 Oscars.

The couple was back on our screens with Nimrods, and Mckenna’s next flick, The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping, is dropping any day now. Which means the time has come to review Mckenna and Mason’s relationship timeline from the very beginning. Starting with...

According to Mason, he and Mckenna first meet in the back of an Uber to see Deadpool & Wolverine in theaters.

“From the moment I met her, we were in this Uber going to see, I think, Deadpool & Wolverine,” he later tells People in October 2025. “And instead of it just being super, super-duper awkward for a first meeting, it was so funny and so awesome.”

He adds, “We just clicked immediately, and ever since then, we just hung out all the time.”

The cast for Regretting You—the film adaptation of Colleen Hoover’s novel of the same name—is announced, with Mckenna and Mason set to star as the film’s romantic leads.

Soon after, the two make their first public appearance together at Netflix’s Incoming House Party event.

Mckenna drops a sweet video with Mason at a Green Day concert. Perhaps it’s prep for their first movie together, Nimrods…

Believe it or not, Nimrods (previously named New Year’s Rev) started filming before Regretting You in February 2025, making it Mason and Mckenna’s first movie together!

Filming begins in Atlanta...and presumably, sparks start flying.

Mckenna oh-so-casually drops this TikTok of a playfully staged kiss between herself and Mason, which has fans like ??????

Not long after, they’re spotted together at the Miami Heat vs. Atlanta Hawks NBA game with their Regretting You costar Dave Franco.

In May, Mckenna posts a photo dump on IG, which includes a few cute candid pics of herself and Mason. Swiiiipe:

Mckenna and Mason first spark dating rumors after they’re photographed holding hands while spending time together in New York City. I mean...

Later, Mason chats with Seventeen and has nothing but good things to say about Mckenna, calling her “one of the most driven people I’ve ever met in my life” and “one of the funniest people I’ve ever met.”

When asked how it feels to have so many people speculating about their relationship (particularly after *that* TikTok), he says, “Yeah, it is strange. But I’ve always sort of been off the internet. I’ve always kind of kept everything to myself and been very personal. Having people speculate so much, especially having mutual friends texting me and asking…it’s wild. I’m still not used to it.”

Mason celebrates his birthday...with Grace! As he explains to People, “I was home back in Texas and I got like a little birthday week and Mckenna was also there, and that was really nice. We spent that together and I spent some time with my family and some of my best friends, and it was nice. It was chill.”

Of Mckenna, he adds, “She’s so funny and such a nice person, so she gels with everybody.” Asked about their experience filming together, he continues to gush, “I've never met somebody with a work ethic like hers. She pushes herself every day harder and harder and she’s so incredibly talented. So just watching that, it really motivates me.”

Plus, Mckenna keeps relationship rumors alive with this cute TikTok:

The two stars attend a screening for Regretting You and have lots of sweet moments, like this one. Ahem:

Mckenna is asked about that April TikTok during an appearance on Today with Jenna & Friends, with Jenna Bush Hager straight-up saying, “So he’s your partner in the film, but is he also kind of your partner?”

And while she initially dodges the question with a “no comment,” Mckenna eventually says, “I love Mason. He’s my favorite person.”

Plus, the Regretting You press tour begins, leading to plenty of moments like these:

After nearly a year of dating rumors, Mason takes to Instagram to share pics of himself and Mckenna vacationing in Paris—including one photo where Mckenna is kissing him on the cheek in front of the Eiffel Tower.

Mckenna then slides into the comments section to write, “this is my favorite post.. for unbiased reasons.” Ooh-là-là!

In case you have any doubts left about Mckenna and Mason’s relationship status, she fully brings him as her date to the 2026 Oscars, where they share lots of cute moments like this one:

Also! Here’s some fun BTS moments from Mckenna:

Mckenna celebrates her 20th birthday with her man...and a few other friends.

Then it’s Mason’s birthday, and he and Mckenna celebrate with the sweetest duo of Olivia Rodrigo’s “honeybee.”

Nimrods hits theaters, giving us even more of these two together on the big screen.

Mason also chats with E! News and reveals how he and Mckenna first found out they were doing back-to-back movies together.

“I was filming How to Train Your Dragon 1 and I had signed on to Green Day maybe a couple of weeks before,” he recalled. “I didn’t think she was signed on. It wasn’t even a thought in my mind. Then I signed on to Regretting You with Mckenna. I’d met her once before, so I texted her and I was like, ‘Hey, super excited to work with you. It’s gonna be fun.’”

He continued, “She was like, ‘Yeah, I’m so excited for Green Day.’ I was like, ‘What? What are you talking about? Green Day?’ So we found out we were doing two movies back-to-back. It was a wild, crazy, weird coincidence.”

Oh, and he also says this of his connection with Mckenna: “We say all the time, if we hadn’t met before, and we met on the day and were shooting a movie, our chemistry would be the same. That just goes with how easy it is to work with her.”

Mckenna drops the music video for her new song “Bones and All”—and surprise! It stars her real-life BF.

During her Cosmo cover story interview (hi!), Mckenna gushes about her connection with Mason, whom she refers to as her “best friend.”

“Your girl is happy,” she says of her love life. “I’m feeling very lovey-dovey. I have somebody I can be a complete nerd with. I got my best friend, and so I don’t see the point in not loving loudly.”

She adds, “There are moments that are just for you and your person. But I don’t see the point in not celebrating who you’re in love with. A lot of people’s hesitation around this stems from the idea that if you post about someone a bunch and then you break up, that’s embarrassing. But why would I even think about that? I’m just going to focus on the present.”

And she concludes with, “I am the biggest fan of the person I’m with, so why wouldn’t I be like, ‘This is my person. I am so stoked I have this’?” The cutest!

More on these two and their budding relationship as we have it.

#6917. This California Cottage Puts a Charming Spin on the "Summer Camp Nostalgia" Aesthetic

Nostalgia rules in this 1930s Northern California home.

When Corine Maggio and her husband bought their 1930s cottage in Mill Valley, California, it was meant to be their starter home. Coming in at about 1,500 square feet, with an awkwardly laid-out kitchen, living room, and entryway, it didn’t seem to be an ideal place to raise their two children—that was, until they realized how rare it was to have a view, a yard, and walkability. Before they knew it, they were falling in love with their home—and all the potential it held.

“The house has an energy you can’t just recreate,” Maggio explains. With three bedrooms and two bathrooms, there was technically enough room for their family of four, and the architectural bones could easily meld with the relaxed, “summer camp in Maine” aesthetic Maggio chases in every project. “We needed to get smart about space planning and bring back a significant amount of character that had been stripped away,” she adds. In other words, the layout had to be revamped, and nearly every wall needed a dose of paneling, paint, or wallpaper—or preferably, some combination of the three.

The kitchen was first on the chopping block. Sandwiched between the laundry room and the dining room, the space felt cut off from the rest of the house in a way that wasn’t conducive to their family-focused, indoor-outdoor lifestyle. Maggio’s solution was to remove the laundry room wall, giving the kitchen access to the backyard, and finish the space with a painted beadboard ceiling to lend it a rustic, timeworn feel.

Mixed patterns, layered textures, and natural materials became Maggio’s way of capturing that summer camp nostalgia without creating a space that felt too formal for everyday life. The narrow living room lent itself well to a circular seating arrangement; when paired with a canopy-inspired wallcovering, the layout mirrored the act of sitting around a campfire to tell stories. Throughout, vintage furniture mingles with custom pieces, and personal touches abound, from the butcher block Maggio found on Facebook Marketplace to a chair left behind by a previous owner.

“I want people to come in and feel something familiar,” Maggio says. “I want them to feel comfortable being themselves, knowing nothing in the house is more precious than the people.”

FAST FACTSDesigner: Corine MaggioLocation: Mill Valley, CaliforniaThe Space: A 1930 cottage with three bedrooms and two bathrooms.

Located in the center of the action—and boasting the best views of the water—this multifunctional space was designed as a place for family and guests to gather while Maggio and her husband cook.

After reviewing more than 30 wallpaper samples, Maggio finally selected this woodland scene by Bunny Williams Home, her least favorite of the bunch until she held it up in the space and saw the transformation it would make.

To visually lighten the galley kitchen, upper cabinets flanking the Ilve range were fitted with glass, breaking up the run of solid cabinetry and creating a more open feel. The butcher-block island is from the 1940s and weighs over 400 pounds.

Originally, there wasn’t a proper mudroom, only a three-walled entry open to the elements. Maggio enclosed it, painting the new beadboard-covered walls in Swans Mill Cream by Benjamin Moore, a cheery yellow that glows in the sunlight.

Maggio’s home office is a detached Tuff Shed in the backyard, to which she added paneling and Soane Britain wallpaper for an English garden vibe. Pinned-up magazine pages on the wall serve as both inspiration and art.

Maggio’s daughter, June, “personifies joy,” so she wanted her retreat to promote imagination and fun. Filled with vintage pieces and clad in a Pierre Frey wallpaper, it feels nostalgic and youthful.

To accentuate the room’s vaulted ceiling, Maggio switched from vertical nickel groove panels to horizontal ones, carrying them up to the room’s peak.

In a controversial move, the designer reduced the room’s square footage and opted for one sink instead of two. “We benefit significantly more from the counter space and storage,” she says. A vintage dresser gets new life as a vanity.

When Corine Maggio started her eponymous design firm, she knew she wanted to create homes for her clients that felt warm, character-rich, and deeply personal. Her goal isn’t to simply design spaces that look beautiful and collected, but to improve how people feel in their daily lives.

#6916. Pamela Anderson’s Cranberry Nails Are the Most Elegant Fall Manicure: How to Recreate

Not only is the shape practical, but the color is timeless.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

I said it before, and I’ll say it again: Pamela Anderson has become one of the most unexpected beauty muses. From her pared-back approach to makeup to her ever-changing hairstyles, the actor has spent recent years setting trends that span generations. And her latest manicure is making a rather convincing case for swapping your usual autumnal burgundy for something a little juicier.

Taking to Instagram, Anderson recently shared a video reel documenting her recent evening spent gardening, reading, and simply living her best life. "Fall is the season of the soul… 🤍🍂," she wrote to caption the post. While the wholesome content had me questioning my own screen-time report (spoiler: it’s an unhealthy amount), it was her nails that left a lasting impression on the beauty editor in me. And she’s been rocking a versatile fall hue everywhere from her backyard to the CFDA Awards.

A far cry from her signature French manicure, Anderson debuted a glossy, cranberry-red shade painted onto short, squoval-shaped nails. Not only is the shape practical and universally flattering, but the color is timeless and an autumn manicure classic.

To recreate Pamela Anderson’s cranberry squoval nails, you’re going to want to invest some time into the prep. I’m talking trimming your cuticles, filing your nails down, and rounding the corners off for that signature square-meets-oval shape.

Then comes the polish. A bright-toned red like essie’s Forever Yummy or OPI’s Big Apple Red is going to be your best bet. Finish with a generous helping of a high-shine top coat for that freshly picked, almost wet-gloss look, and you’re good to go!

Who knew a trip down the fruit and vegetable aisle could double as a nail appointment? I’ll definitely be channeling the duality of Anderson both gardening and attending a chic red carpet with this fall-ready manicure.

#6912. I’m a Tech-Obsessed Editor—Here’s How to Score the Best AirPods Deals for Black Friday

Apple’s most popular headphones are already 20% off.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Savvy shoppers already know that Apple never hosts its own sales—so if you’re looking for a discounted price, Amazon is *the* place to score AirPods. With Black Friday sales on the horizon, I’m already seeing decent markdowns on Apple’s most popular headphones—and I’ll be tracking the best prices until the holiday weekend arrives on November 27.

What’s worth shopping right now? The AirPods Pro 3 are already 20% off, and the AirPods Max 2 are $100 off. Keep reading for my honest opinions on what you should grab now, and what may be cheaper in a few weeks.

Right now, the best AirPods deal on Amazon is 20% off the AirPods 3. These best-selling buds are packed with premium features: they’ve got fantastic active noise cancellation, a built-in heart rate sensor, and even a live language translation feature that’s handy during travel. They also have a decent battery life compared to other AirPods models, with up to 8 hours of listening time on a single charge.

As for the deal itself? I’ve seen similar prices on the AirPods 3 during smaller sales like Labor Day and Memorial Day, so this isn’t necessarily a jaw-dropping deal. This exact pair dropped as low as 32% off during Prime Day—so we’re likely to see a better deal in the coming weeks. Personally, I’d hold off until Black Friday gets closer for the best price possible.

Prefer an over-the-ear headphone? Right now, you can save $100 on the AirPods Max 2. Similar to the AirPods 3, this discount isn’t the best I’ve ever seen—they dropped to a record-low price of $399 during June Prime Day. I expect to see a similar (or even better!) deal during Amazon’s official Black Friday sale.

This splurge-worthy model is a favorite of It Girls like Bella Hadid, Dua Lipa, and Hailey Bieber for its sleek design, and it’s available in a range of colors from pearly beige to lilac. It’s equipped with padded ear cups and a breathable mesh headband for all-day comfort, plus active noise canceling and stellar audio quality. What’s more, it has the best battery life out of Apple’s headphone lineup, with up to 20 hours of listening time per charge.

While I’m impressed to see two popular AirPods on sale this far ahead of Black Friday, I still think it’s worth holding out for a better deal. Plus, I predict that we might see a first-ever discount on brand-new models like the AirPods 5. TL;DR: Watch this space!

#6907. This Heritage Pattern Is Replacing Flat, Beige Walls—Here’s How Designers Are Using It in 2026

The centuries-old motif also makes lampshades, rugs, and seating feel bespoke.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

In an age of encroaching AI, designers are turning to heritage looks to create bespoke interiors with personality. That may be one reason decorative marbling is having a moment.

"People are tired of flat, safe walls,” says designer Cara Woodhouse. “After years of greige, clients want surfaces with a point of view, and marbling has an artisanal history behind it. It's also flexible: it can read painterly, graphic, or soft depending on the colorway."

Its versatility is part of the appeal. “It doesn't necessarily have to be traditional or even executed on paper,” adds designer Sasha Bikoff. “It's really about the movement of color and the organic, unpredictable patterns that emerge, which can translate beautifully across wallpaper, furniture, textiles, and decorative objects.”

The paper marbling technique has a long history. In 10th-century Japan, artisans created “floating ink” designs by placing ink on water and manipulating it as it moved. By the 15th century, the practice was known in Turkey as “the art of clouds,” before making its way to Venetian and Florentine bookbinders.

The painterly look is appealing because its undulating lines feel expressive and vibrant without harsh edges, says Jo Plant, chief creative officer at Pooky, which is a brand well-known for its marbled lampshades.

Designers are bringing marbling into all kinds of interiors, from murals and upholstery to lampshades and even sink skirts. Analisse Taft-Gersten, cofounder of the 1818 Collective, used billowy marbled silk to elevate a powder room. And studios like Rule of Three are experimenting with the technique in floral fabrics that resemble vines.

Should you need further convincing, Woodhouse puts it best: "Marbling is pattern without repetition. No two passes are the same, so a wall reads more like art than wallpaper. It brings depth and a handmade quality to a room that might otherwise feel too finished."

Here are nine designer-approved ways to bring marbled motifs into your home.

Designer Michelle Fahmy of Haus of Meeshie likes things cheeky and playful. So it was a no-brainer to introduce groovy swirls into a Culver City pied-à-terre via a large-scale mural from Mitchell Black.

“I wanted something dramatic for the stairwell wall which bleeds into the living room and is the first thing you see when you walk in the door,” she says. “When I came across this wallpaper, it stopped me in my tracks! Usually, you see this paper marbling technique done on fabric or in smaller applications, like a lampshade, so this was unusual in scale and exactly the conversation starter I was looking for.”

For Bikoff, interior design should feel like a natural extension of a home’s architectural language. To bring her own sense of color, movement, and personality into a project within a Zaha Hadid building, she opted for Pierre Frey wallpaper with a liquid, dripping marble effect rather than a traditional, tightly swirled pattern.

“I love how the elongated, flowing lines create a sense of movement, almost as though the walls are melting or the colors are cascading down the surface,” Bikoff says. “Traditional marbling can feel very classical, whereas this interpretation feels more contemporary, psychedelic, and expressive.”

The dialogue between the wallpaper and Hadid's architecture is all about challenging conventional forms and embracing organic movement. “I wanted the room to feel immersive, almost like stepping inside a piece of abstract art,” she adds.

It was love at first sight. Marta St. Amour, interior designer and project manager at Braswell Design + Build was enchanted by the beautiful movement and layered tones of amber, soft blue, and sandy beige portrayed in the Lee Jofa Taplow wallpaper.

“The pattern became the inspiration for the bathroom's color palette, tying together the warm veining of Taj Mahal quartzite, rich caramel-colored shower tile, and soft blue tile behind the freestanding tub,” she says.

“We wanted the tub to feel like a statement piece, and the wallpaper's organic, flowing pattern complements its sculptural shape and warm metallic finish.”

To capture the spirit of the marbling technique but in a contemporary way, Bikoff opted for resin chairs with the telltale pattern in the above space.

“The way the colors blend and swirl through the resin gives them this incredible sense of depth and movement, almost like a liquid material that has been frozen in time,” she says.

Juxtaposing materials can challenge the perception of design. Here, the chairs feel like functional sculptures as they balance the visual richness of marble and the playful transparency of resin.

“They bring together color, texture, and fluidity while reinforcing the playful, futuristic spirit of the space."

If a full room of swirls is a bit much for you, settle on something smaller-scale, such as a lampshade. Pooky’s hand-marbled shades show off the dance of acrylic paint.

“Marbling is having a resurgence because we’re all craving craft, texture, and character in our homes,” says Plant. “People want pieces that feel human and handmade rather than mass-produced.” Adding a marbled shade is like decor candy that can add just the right amount of maximalism.

What makes the marbling technique unique is that the patterns are inherently organic, and so they lend themselves to pairing well with other organic shapes. Taft-Gersten anchored this powder room with a silk sink skirt featuring Rule of Three fabric that almost looks like it was kissed by bubbles.

“There’s a rhythm to it, but also an irregularity that keeps your eye moving; it never feels rigid,” Taft-Gersten says. The print also echoes the convex pebble mirrors above.

This girl’s bedroom may look dreamy, but that’s not what Cara Woodhouse was after when she landed on cotton candy-like Merenda Wallpaper by artist Michelle Weinberg.

“I wanted movement,” Woodhouse says. “A kid's room should feel alive, and marbled paper gives the walls energy without a single literal motif. I'd call it soft modern with an edge: rounded forms, blush tones, and a pattern that keeps it from going saccharine. The swirl does the work so the furniture can stay simple and sculptural."

The look of poured paint over a rug is one that will surely raise an eyebrow. Floor_Story designer Bekah Smith was experimenting with duct tape when she stumbled upon unexpected patterns that look like creases and shadows.

“There’s a similar sense of spontaneity to marbling, where patterns emerge through experimentation rather than being entirely predetermined,” Smith says.

For those looking for a ripple effect without the psychedelic swirls, consider ceramics as a way to dabble in the style without being so literal. Henry Holland Studio employs a traditional Japanese ceramic technique, nerikomi, to create irregular earthenware marbled motifs.

The pattern is created by stacking, folding, and rolling colored clay into slabs, then hand-molding them into functional shapes like lamp bases, bowls, and vases.

#6906. The Author Behind the Pseudonym Rebecca Morrow Was a Total Mystery—Until Now

Rainbow Rowell is the bestselling writer behind the novel Corinne. Here, she explains why it was finally time to come forward.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

In 2022, the novel Corinne was published. At the story’s center is Corinne, a woman who walked away from family and her fundamentalist upbringing but finds herself unable to forget Enoch, her first love. Rebecca Morrow, the name on the book’s cover, was described as a “pseudonym for a New York Times bestselling author,” and though readers guessed at who that author might be, Rebecca Morrow’s identity remained a mystery until now.

Rebecca Morrow is Rainbow Rowell, the celebrated author of Cherry Baby and Eleanor & Park, among others. Corinne isn’t about her life, but it has parallels, and in 2022, publishing something so personal felt impossible. Now denying what she has come to consider her favorite book seems unthinkable. In July 2027, Corinne will be reissued with Rowell’s name on the cover, and in 2028 she will publish Catch Us the Foxes, a Corinne sequel.

Here Rowell talks about the decision to write Corinne—and deciding to finally put her name on it—in her own words.

In the darkest hours of the COVID-19 pandemic—when the future seemed uncertain, and the rest of the world felt far away—I did something very strange for me.

I wrote a book, knowing that I couldn’t let anyone read it.

“I just need to do this,” I told my literary agent. “And then I’ll write something we can sell.” It was a good time to sell. I’d just published three No. 1 bestsellers in a row. “I just need to get this out of me first.”

I wrote the first draft of the novel almost feverishly. It was about a woman who is cast out of her strict Christian church as a teenager and cut off by her family. She comes back home as an adult to reckon with the past—and with the man who was the catalyst for her exile.

Really, the book was about all the things I’d always been too scared to write about, or even to talk about….Faith. Shame. Sex. (More sex. Unclean sex. Forbidden sex. Sex in the same room as a Bible.) And, most thornily, how it feels to leave a fundamentalist church. The way you eventually realize that you can’t leave. That you’ll always take it with you, because you were made there. Baked there. Poured and set.

When I was done with the manuscript, it was my favorite thing I’d ever written. Maybe not my best book, but already the one I loved the most.

“But honestly,” I told my agent, “we can’t do anything with this. It’s too personal. And it’s the very last thing any of my readers would want.” I was writing queer YA fantasy. Not whatever this was.

My agent, being an agent, asked if he could read it anyway.

And he liked it. (He liked my beautiful baby!)

We very quickly began talking about a way to publish the book that might sidestep all my fears and anxieties….

There began my strange—and still hardly believable to me—journey as Rebecca Morrow, the pseudonymous author of Corinne.

It was exciting at first. It was different and fun. A pseudonym! A secret identity! A way to drop a novel into the world without having to talk about it, or sell it. Without having to worry about alienating or recruiting my existing readers.

The pseudonym satisfied that still feverish part of me that needed to let the story out, but was too terrified to stand by it.

Oh, I knew it was absurd to write a book about shame and secrets, and then to keep it a secret. (Don’t think this didn’t come up in therapy!) But at the time, it seemed like the only path forward for Corinne. It was this or let the characters die on my hard drive.

My editor and I agonized over how to handle the pseudonym. Traditionally, authors write fake biographies to go with their fake names. But I didn’t want to get caught in a lie if I was found out. And I didn’t want to seek out undeserved goodwill as a “debut author.”

The marketing department really wanted something they could say about Rebecca Morrow. They suggested “lives in Nebraska,” but even that felt like divulging too much. We settled on “Rebecca Morrow is a pseudonym for a New York Times bestselling author.”

My apologies to the bestselling, usually Mormon, authors who got blamed for this weird book. I didn’t see that coming.

I did expect readers to guess that I was the author. Even though I’d freed myself to write Corinne in a new voice and shaken off some of my more recognizable habits, the book ended up sounding more like me than ever. It was like my voice distilled.

Some people did guess. It didn’t bother me. I still didn’t have to talk about the book. I didn’t have to face it.

Corinne is my favorite of my books and beyond that, Enoch and Corinne, the man and woman at its center, are my all-time favorite characters. It’s hard to explain why…

I always write messy, flawed characters, but these two are my messiest and most flawed. Their love is not the stuff of romance. (This novel is not a romance; it will disappoint you if you come to it looking for reassurance or escape.)

Neither Corinne nor Enoch is based on me, though they carry many of my burdens. This is not a hidden memoir. But they feel so real to me. They make mistakes, and I want to forgive them. I want them to find grace. I think about them still, all the time. I worry about what’s become of them.

Ultimately, that’s why I’m stepping forward to claim the book now. At some point, “not having to talk about Corinne” turned into “not being able to talk about Corinne,” even when I desperately wanted to.

I love this book! I want to put it on my shelf, between Any Way the Wind Blows and Scattered Showers. I want readers who liked Slow Dance and Cherry Baby to discover it.

I loved the characters in Corinne so much that I wrote a sequel about them—Catch Us the Foxes—and I want that book to have a life, too. William Morrow is launching both books, properly—Corinne next July, and Catch Us the Foxes in March 2028—in beautiful hardcover editions.

I revised Corinne for the new edition—I went a little deeper on the church stuff this time, and sharpened the focus on some of the characters. It’s a little longer. And, I think, a little better. My favorite designer, Jim Tierney, designed the new matching covers.

I still get a surge of fear and excitement every time I see my name there front and center.

My therapist says that I’m very good at compartmentalizing—keeping some parts of myself shut away from other parts. I would argue that this is a very useful skill! But it does bad things to your soul, over time...being fractured like that.

But as the years have passed, it’s felt like I’ve left something half done. Like Corinne is a truth half shared. A story half told. I needed to get the book out of me, but I ended up cutting myself off from it. That was never the goal.

I’m grateful to “Rebecca Morrow” for making Corinne possible, but I’m finally ready to bring these characters home.

From Sabrina Carpenter as Hairspray’s Penny to Kendall Jenner’s Paris, Texas moment

Another Halloween means another round of celebrity costumes. #6902. Why Everyone Is Wearing Suede This Fall—And the Chicest Ways to Style It

When the temperatures drop and fall dressing takes center stage, one fabric always finds its way back into our wardrobes: suede. Suede has been that fall fashion staple ever since it has been around. It brings that cozy, elevated feel to even the simplest outfits, making it the perfect texture to embrace when the seasons change.

While it’s common to see suede generally in the fall, lately it’s absolutely everywhere, and I’m here to remind you that there are so many ways you can wear this texture. From suede mini skirts paired with oversized sweaters, knee-high suede boots, and of course purses.

Suede is such a perfect fall trend because it brings a subtle touch of texture to any outfit. Shades like chocolate brown and deep burgundy make the material feel especially autumnal.

Consider this your sign to give your fall wardrobe a suede refresh. Whether you go all in with a statement skirt or keep things simple with a chic suede bag, this is one trend that makes every outfit feel a little more luxurious. And if you need some inspiration, I’ve got you covered with some trendy looks below.

There is something so effortlessly chic about a suede skirt. It’s a fun piece I’d wear to work, but styled like the above makes for such a cute day or night time look. The addition of the colored tights and shoes elevates the whole moment, so if you’re a maximalist this suede look is for you.

The simplest way to tap into the suede trend is with your footwear. There are so many popular suede shoes meant for the fall and winter season and these boots are the perfect ones.

Even though I am not a big hat girl, a suede hat like this is a fun way to test the waters. It’s easy and adds to any fall outfit you’re building.

Another staple way to tap into suede is with accessories, specifically a good purse. This dark brown suede shoulder bag is so stylish and paired with the all-white outfit is a texture combo I’m obsessing over.

Of course, no fall wardrobe is complete without a statement jacket. Suede blazers, like the one above, add instant personality to your everyday looks. They’re the kind of investment pieces that make getting dressed feel exciting, even on the busiest mornings.

Aiyana Ishmael is the style editor at Cosmopolitan magazine. In her work, Aiyana focuses on the culture of fashion and how it intertwines and shapes the zeitgeist. She is an award-winning journalist from Miami, Florida, and a graduate of the historically Black university, Florida A&M. She is a 2024 Forbes 30 Under 30: Media honoree, and her debut romance novella PASSING GAME is set to release March of 2027 (831 Stories/Simon & Schuster).

#6901. Deer Print Is Replacing Leopard This Autumn

And Mango’s $90 barrel jeans are the perfect way to wear it.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

If you ask us, animal print should be considered a neutral: a pattern so timeless and versatile, it deserves a permanent home in your forever wardrobe, alongside those blue jeans, white tees, and black boots.

And while zebra stripes and cheetah spots may come and go, there’s no more ubiquitous animal pattern than leopard print. Since its 1980s heyday, fashion’s perennial favorite has been reimagined countless times, leaning either ultra-glam or rebelliously punk, depending on how you wear it.

But for FW26, the spots are finally changing. While we’re absolutely not decreeing the demise of leopard (our love will never wane), there’s now a new pattern breathing fresh life into our autumnal wardrobes.

Deer print—or, to use its other moniker, Bambi print—is the pattern to know this season, appearing across ready-to-wear and accessories from one end of the street to the other. The easiest entry route may be a pair of calf-hair ballet flats or a working-week tote, but the most impactful approach? That has to be Mango’s stellar pair of balloon-leg jeans, which are destined to replace that leopard-print pair we know you were wearing all weekend long.

With a voluminous curved leg, this frankly excellent pair of statement jeans ticks off two major FW26 trends in one—and they’re incredibly flattering to boot (if you haven’t tried this silhouette yet, now’s the time). Priced at $90, they’re already selling out fast, so we suggest you move swiftly.

What to wear them with? Really, the options are plentiful. All the classics will work here: your white T-shirt, gray cardigan, cozy black sweater. Leather looks particularly chic—consider a funnel-neck jacket or slick black accessories. Sneakers and color-pop socks are your weekend go-to, of course. For smarter occasions, a dark taupe-brown lipstick will lend the look a little glamour, as will a pair of chunky silver earrings. And on your feet? Ballet flats now (a pointed style is an especially good move) and block-heel boots once the cold really sets in.

#6900. Winter Date Ideas to Heat Up Your Love Life This Season

Time to turn those dark, cold nights into hot date nights.

I’m afraid the rumors are true: Winter is, in fact, upon us. But while the colder weather may find you ready to cuddle up with takeout and TV ‘til spring, let this be your reminder that winter does not mean you have to put your dating life on hold. While this may not be the sexiest time of year, it is the coziest—which means that the cooler months ahead are filled with romantic date-night potential. To help you make the most of that potential, allow me to present this list of the best winter date ideas for you and whoever you’re cuddling up to this season.

Whether you’re already happily cuffed and committed, single and seeking, or feeling things out with a new crush, there’s something on this list for every kind of winter date vibe. Prepare to fill your cold-weather calendar with these 18 winter date ideas.

What better way to heat up your winter nights than an evening of laughs with the one you love? And by the way, you don’t have to shell out for a Netflix Special taping (although if you can swing it, by all means). Tons of venues in cities across the country regularly host affordable standup shows where you can grab some food or a drink and enjoy a chill night filled with plenty of laughs.

To be clear, you can support the arts any time. But there’s just something a little fancy-yet-cozy about winter ballet.

In a similar if much sexier vein, nothing beats a burlesque date night. If you’re New York–based (or have an NYC trip coming up this holiday season) I literally can’t recommend Company XIV’s Nutcracker Rouge highly enough. Get tickets here!

Can you technically see a play any time of year? Yes, of course. But you can’t tell me there’s not something about “going to the theater” that just seems to ooze a certain wintery sophistication. Whether it’s a Broadway show or a more intimate performance at your local theater, catching some live entertainment is a perfect way to spend a winter night out on the town.

If you’re down to class things up a bit this winter, nothing will make you feel more sexy and sophisticated than a jazz club date night—just you and your date, sipping cocktails and enjoying some live tunes.

If you and your person are already seasoned slope-hitters, then a ski trip is a given. But if you don’t know your way around the slopes yet, why not take a lesson together? It’s a great opportunity to learn something new, get outside, and make the most of the cold weather—and it comes with a built-in excuse to warm up with a steaming mug of hot chocolate (or your après-ski beverage of choice) afterwards.

Sometimes an intimate evening with a home-cooked meal you prepare together is actually more romantic than dining out. Pick a recipe you both want to try, open a bottle of wine, and channel your inner chef.

Plenty of companies now offer virtual wine tastings you can do from the comfort of your own home. They send the wine to you, and you and your date can follow along as an expert guides you through the various selections in a Zoom-style seminar. What better way to spend a night in than exploring new wines together and refining your sommelier sensibilities?

Alexa, play “Christmas Tree Farm” by Taylor Swift. Whether or not you actually want to perform the manual labor of cutting down a live tree, lugging it home, and setting it up when you could just order a fake from Amazon, spending a cozy day at a Christmas tree farm is an iconic holi-date. It’s outdoorsy without being too physically demanding, it’s brimming with cute selfie potential, and you can basically pretend you’re in the cheesy-yet-addictive holiday rom-com of your dreams. What more could you ask for?

Get into the holiday spirit and cross some seasonal chores off your to-do list by turning holiday decorating into a cute date night. Best of all: You get to stay inside and keep your comfy clothes on.

This one may sound a little less fun, but hear me out. Un-decorating is sad and boring and work—but turning it into date night can make an otherwise tedious chore actually kind of fun! Grab some wine, throw on a movie or set the mood with some music, and un-deck the halls.

While some of your summertime haunts may be closed for the season, plenty of typically summery destinations are actually open year-round. Not to mention, a lot of them are probably less expensive and less crowded during the off-season. Plan a trip to your favorite beach town or seaside locale and see what it’s like in the winter. Chances are it’s probably a lot more peaceful.

Spending a night together in a hotel is a great way to get out of the house and treat yourselves to a romantic mini-getaway. Some hotels even offer special holiday/winter packages for the season.

Nothing says cuffing season like meeting the parents! But even if you’re not ready to take that step, a little trip to your hometown to show your crush all the old haunts is a great way to spend a winter weekend and indulge in a little nostalgia.

Sometimes it can be tough to work up the motivation to actually get up and go out in the winter. It’s dark, it’s cold, your seasonal depression is acting up, etc. But that’s exactly why you deserve to treat yourselves to a luxe night out. You know that fancy restaurant you’ve been saving for a special occasion? Screw the special occasion. Go now, for no reason other than you can.

What better way to unwind and beat the winter blues than a spa date? Treat yourselves to a couple’s massage, a relaxing sauna steam, and/or some beauty treatments that your dry winter skin will definitely thank you for.

Lots of bars offer mixology classes where you and your date can not only enjoy some delicious seasonal cocktails but also learn to make them yourselves. If you’d rather not leave the house, there are also virtual options you can attend online from the comfort of your own home.

Going to the movies is a lost art. Catch a new release (Wuthering Heights, anyone?) and enjoy your date’s company while snacking on popcorn, candy, and slurpees

#6898. 15 Paint Colors HGTV Stars Swear By for a Home That Looks Expensive

From sea blue to crisp white, these designer favorites are timeless.

Everyone knows that a quick coat of paint is the fastest way to transform a room. It’s a favorite trick among professional designers and DIYers alike, but choosing the perfect shade isn't always easy. With hundreds of colors to choose from, finding “the one” can quickly become overwhelming. If you’re feeling a little stuck, don’t fret: HGTV’s biggest stars make these tough decisions every day.

Whether they’re renovating an outdated home or putting the finishing touches on a dream build, HGTV personalities are constantly selecting paint colors that make a space feel fresh, spacious, inviting, and beautifully designed. Over the years, they’ve crowned a select few as go-to favorites that are foolproof and versatile enough to work in almost any room. When every renovation comes with deadlines and budgets, dependable paint colors become invaluable.

To help narrow your search, we asked some of our favorite HGTV stars to reveal the paint colors they reach for most often. Their picks include everything from versatile neutrals to powdery pastels that work across a variety of homes and aesthetics. Additional copy by Alyssa Khan.

“We love muted colors because they’re playful, but not over-the-top. Behr Mocha Ice is a perfect example of that balance—it brings the personality you need to take a space from basic and bland to warm and interesting. Try it in a bedroom, dining room, or anywhere that’s missing that ‘wow’ factor.”—Drew Scott, Property Brothers

“This beautiful blue is timeless and versatile. It’s calming, but not boring. Even though Behr's Dragonfly makes a statement, it’s still neutral enough for many spaces, whether it’s on every wall in a bedroom or used on a specific feature like kitchen cabinets.”—Jonathan Scott, Property Brothers

“This never-boring black paint color is dramatic and impactful yet very versatile. Pair with white for a timeless aesthetic. Use it to create a dramatic accent wall or outside for your exterior (like we did) or trim for maximum curb appeal.”—Tarek El Moussa, The Flip Off

“My favorite paint color is Tricorn Black by Sherwin-Williams. It’s bold, clean, and makes every detail pop. Whether it’s a door, a wall, or literally anything else, this black brings that high-end look every single time. Never fails.”—Cole DeBoer, Down Home Fab

“I am currently loving greige, the perfect blend of gray and beige. It’s warm, soft, and timeless—a great option for homeowners who want both cozy and modern. Benjamin Moore's Revere Pewter is a classic and rich greige with warmth. Great for open-concept homes.”—Heather Rae El Moussa, The Flip Off

“What I love most about it is its confidence. Studio Green by Farrow & Ball doesn’t whisper: It commands. It’s deep, moody, and unapologetically rich, the kind of color that instantly elevates a space and gives it a point of view. In daylight, it reads as a classic, almost architectural green, grounded and refined. But at night? It transforms into something velvety and dramatic, wrapping the room in a quiet luxury that feels both intimate and powerful.”—Egypt Sherrod, Married to Real Estate andProperty Virgins

“Benjamin Moore Misty Air OC-44 is my constant when I need a bright neutral. It is a broken-in cream that’s the perfect backdrop for colorful art and upholstery.”—Erin Napier, Home Town

““My current favorite paint color is Black Fox by Sherwin-Williams. It’s the perfect mix of sophisticated and moody, with just enough warmth to make any space feel grounded and timeless. It pairs beautifully with so many other tones and textures. I love it in an office!”—Chelsea DeBoer, Down Home Fab

Magnolia Home by Joanna Gaines Juniper Tree Crafted by KILZ

“Seed & Supply was one of the first additions we made to the Silos back in 2015. Before it served as our garden shop on the grounds, it was used as an additional retail space at the original Magnolia shop, which we lovingly refer to as The Little Shop on Bosque. This year, the team had the idea to evolve the building yet again, but this time to serve as a creative workshop space for our guests. I immediately loved the idea. It has always been my hope that our guests’ experience at the Silos would inspire creativity and connection.

The whole interior of the workshop is refreshed with one color, Juniper Tree Trim, Door, and Cabinetry Paint in satin finish. I love how these nature-forward hues turned out—they feel fresh yet familiar, bold yet comforting.”—Joanna Gaines, Fixer Upper

““White Flour is the perfect soft white backdrop for every style. It accentuates simplicity or maximalist energy. The warm hue is anything but basic.”—Taniya Nayak, Battle on the Beach

“I love Farrow & Ball's Drop Cloth for walls, or for trim and cabinetry with a more true white on the wall. It is a timeless neutral that looks beautiful, particularly in historic homes. This warm neutral evokes calm tranquility without being sterile. It is the perfect first layer for any space: It sets a foundation of simplicity that can easily be layered upon with color on cabinetry, trim detail, furniture, art, and décor.”—Jenny Marrs, Fixer to Fabulous

“I keep going back to Behr White. It’s porcelain-like and a classic white without being too stark or jarring. It also has a very slight undertone of blue, which I prefer over yellow or pink undertones, as a slight hint of blue preserves more of a pure white tone.”—Galey Alix, Home in a Heartbeat With Galey Alix

“My current favorite color is Benjamin Moore Windsor Green. I just painted my bedroom this color! The walls, the doors, the windows, and the trim. It's just so deep and lovely. To me, a deep mossy green is cozy and sophisticated at the same time. It steals the show every time, which is why I went for it in my own bedroom!”—Kristina Crestin, Farmhouse Fixer

In her blog for the Laurel Mercantile, the Home Town Takeover co-host crowned Dover White her all-time favorite hue.

“My all-time favorite white, forever and always, to the point my design team just defaults to it when they aren't able to get a quick decision from me.”— Erin Napier, Home Town Takeover

Fix My Flip star, Page Turner, shared her favorite shade of white with HGTV fans. Boasting warm cream and soft beige undertones, Whisper White is the perfect airy neutral.

“I like how this shade isn’t as stark as other white paints can be.” — Page Turner, Fix My Flip

The Property Brothers named Grenada Villa as one of their go-to shades for their site, drew & jonathan, but fans of Brother vs. Brother will recall the shade being name-dropped much earlier. Drew used it to coat the guest bedroom ceiling in the Season 6 Canyon House in 2018. The serene shade has remained in their palette ever since.

#6897. This Halloween, Hailey and Justin Bieber Are the Angel and Devil on Your Shoulder

Their 2026 couples costume was to die for

Hailey Bieber has always been in support of going all out when it comes to Halloween. Whether dressing up as a Victorian vampire with blood-stained pearls and a lace-covered corset (2023) or pairing up with Kendall Jenner to channel Nicole Richie and Paris Hilton from The Simple Life (2024), she’s committed to the holiday year after year. #6896. Zendaya Recreated an Iconic Halle Berry Fashion Moment for Halloween

Zendaya didn’t miss out on all the fun this Halloween. The actress dressed up as one of Hollywood’s most enduring screen and style icons, Halle Berry, thanks to a special vintage pull.

On Saturday, Zendaya’s longtime stylist, Law Roach, shared a series of photos of the Dune actress recreating a particularly memorable early-aughts look that Berry wore: a butter yellow, brown, and white printed string bikini top with silver accents and matching low-rise, wide-leg trousers from Lanvin’s fall 2000 collection. Zendaya completed the look with pointed-toe heels.

Roach thanked Lanvin’s artistic director Peter Copping and Alix Long in his post. Copping took the creative reins at the French fashion house in 2024, and Cristina Ortiz was Lanvin’s womenswear designer when the fall 2000 collection debuted.

Berry first wore the two-piece to the New York premiere of X-Men in July 2000. She accessorized with a belly chain and tinted sunglasses.

Berry saw the tribute and posted her approval on X. “She won,” Berry wrote of Zendaya.

The fashion homage feels particularly fitting given that Zendaya has been drawing comparisons to Berry since debuting her pixie cut.

In fact, when Zendaya first unveiled the look at the 2026 Emmy Awards in September, Roach confirmed that the haircut was inspired by Berry’s iconic short hairstyle.

Berry later embraced the tribute, sharing Roach’s interview on her Instagram Story and writing that Zendaya “murdered the night.”

This isn’t the first time Zendaya has used fashion to pay tribute to another star. In 2013, she channeled the late Aaliyah for Keep a Child Alive’s Dream Halloween, recreating the singer’s iconic Tommy Hilfiger campaign look. More recently, she honored Cher at the 2024 Rock & Roll Hall of Fame induction ceremony in a vintage Bob Mackie design.

Zendaya and Roach have more fashion moments ahead: The actress’s Dune: Part Three press tour is just kicking off.

#6889. Hailey Bieber and Zoë Kravitz Are Loving This Low-Maintenance Nail Trend for Fall

Here’s how to recreate the goes-with-anything manicure.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Last night’s CFDA Awards brought all of fashion’s coolest people together. With glitzy designer dresses and a star-studded carpet, the event boasted a guest list with A-listers like Kim Kardashian, Demi Moore, Megan Thee Stallion, and more, at the Museum of Natural History in New York City. But amid the luxe labels and prestigious awards, one beauty trend was the common denominator taking over the carpet: glassy nails.

In a sea of fall nail colors like earthy greens and moody burgundies, glassy manicures offer a welcome break from bolder polishes. Celebrity beauty muses like Hailey Bieber, Zoë Kravitz, and more were spotted rocking the barely-there manicure. And the best part of all? The sheer, glossy finish goes with just about everything, making it an easy look to wear all season (or year) long, no matter what you're wearing.

This sheer manicure is so easy to DIY, and it’s also super low-maintenance. As it starts to grow out, unlike a bright or deep hue, you can barely tell where the new growth begins.

Start by grooming and shaping your nails for a clean base. Trim any loose cuticles, buff away uneven texture, and file them into your desired shape. Whether you like your fingertips short and round, or long and almond, this fresh finish looks good on any length (as showcased by the chic celebrities who have been rocking many versions of the look lately).

Once your canvas is prepped and clean, choose your glass polish. You can lean a little milkier and pinker with a shade like Essie’s Skinny Dip, or opt for a nourishing formula like Manucurist’s Active Bright. Or, go for a completely shiny finish with a clear coat of OPI’s Start to Finish. For a more translucent look, use just one coat of your desired polish, and for more opaque finishes, two layers should do the trick.

#6888. Experts Say This Is the Best Way to Get Rid of Fruit Flies—and You Probably Have It in Your Pantry

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Few things are less pleasant than finding insects taking over your kitchen. Fruit flies may not be as alarming as cockroaches or stink bugs, but they’re still a nuisance—and they’re a signal that you’re not cleaning as thoroughly as you should be.

Fruit flies often congregate in sticky, sweet, moist spots: around fresh produce, by the sink, or near the trash. With their persistent buzzing and quick breeding cycle, a few occasional flies can become a much larger problem if you don’t deal with them quickly.

Fortunately, fruit flies are pretty manageable. The first step is to find what they’re drawn to, whether it’s overripe fruit, food spills, or a trash can or compost bin that isn’t sealed properly. Once you’ve found the source, a few straightforward steps can help clear them out and keep the problem from spreading. Homemade traps and smarter food storage are among the practical ways to take back your kitchen.

For expert advice on getting rid of fruit flies, we spoke with Diana Ludwiczak, a certified home pest inspector in New York City. She explains how to deal with an infestation effectively and keep the flies from returning.

“Fruit flies are attracted to ripe and rotting fruits and vegetables, so it’s important to keep your kitchen clean and free of any food debris,” says Ludwiczak. “Wipe down counters, sweep or vacuum the floors regularly, and store fruits and vegetables in the refrigerator to reduce their exposure to the air.”

Fruit flies also like fermented items, including beer, liquor, and wine. They breed in dark, damp areas like sink drains, garbage disposals, trash cans, and mop buckets.

“Once they begin reproducing indoors, females are able to lay about 500 eggs, and the eggs will hatch in as little as 24–30 hours after being deposited by the female,” according to Orkin.

To get rid of fruit flies in your home, you can try this expert-recommended method.

“Fill a small jar or cup with apple cider vinegar and a few drops of dish soap, and then cover the container with plastic wrap tightly,” Ludwiczak suggests. Poke a few holes in the wrap and place the jar near the fruit fly infestation. “The fruit flies will be drawn to the smell and will become trapped in the jar. This is my number one tip, that I personally use every summer!” she adds.

Within a day, you should see many fruit fly carcasses floating in the liquid. You'll want to keep changing out the liquid each day until all the flies have been caught.

Getting rid of fruit flies is just the first step. Here's what Ludwiczak wants you to do to prevent them from coming back.

When you bring home fresh produce from the grocery store, wash it off before storing or displaying it.

“Fruit flies are also attracted to standing water, so it’s important to dry out sinks, showers, and other areas where water can accumulate. Make sure to fix any leaky pipes or areas of your home where water tends to accumulate,” says Ludwiczak.

According to Ludwiczak, rinsing produce with a vinegar solution may keep your produce fresher for longer—and this helps stave off a fruit fly infestation. To make it, Ludwiczak mixes around two parts water and one part vinegar and sprays it onto the produce. Make sure to give your fruits and veggies one more final rinse in water before you’re done, though, so the vinegar flavor and scent don’t stick around.

Screening in your windows is a good idea for preventing all kinds of critters from getting in, but they're also effective against fruit flies.

“Make sure to use screens on windows and doors to prevent fruit flies from entering your home,” Ludwiczak says. “This is especially important during the warmer months when windows and doors are more likely to be open.”

Basic housekeeping is enough to keep fruit flies in check. Just make sure to keep your countertops clean, move overripe fruit to the fridge, take out your garbage regularly, and clean out your sink to prevent it from becoming a breeding ground for bugs.

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

#6884. AO3’s Outage Is Raising Questions About Who Owns the Fics Fans Love So Much

Two to four weeks offline is testing just how much fandom depends on one volunteer-run site.

Archive of Our Own aka AO3 has become the place to spend your time when you’re trying to escape doomscrolling at 1 a.m. and turn to doom-reading instead, as you speed through a fanfiction that would keep you up the rest of the night. But last week, AO3 shocked everyone when it announced it would be down for the next two to four weeks due to a security incident.

For a normal website, this might not be such a big deal. But Archive of Our Own is run by volunteers, so things can’t be fixed as easily. Just as readers yelled out, “Well, what am I supposed to read now?” (A book, maybe?) the community came together to create their own kind of archive of AO3 works, creating folders on Google Docs to share amongst their fellow fans. But there’s a reason as to why this has caused some major issues between the readers who cannot stop consuming these alternative stories and the authors who bring them to life.

For the uninitiated, Archive of Our Own first launched back in 2018 as a site where any and all fanfiction can live. Several other sites made policies to ban certain content—Fanfiction.net has banned some fandoms due to authors’ dislike for fanfiction, Tumblr started to ban explicit content including written works in late 2018, and Wattpad did the same for content that is exclusively “created primarily for sexual arousal”—but AO3 never made rules like that.

For some, this means that there may be content on AO3 that goes against their morals or their ideas of what is okay to consume in media. But, for Archive of Our Own’s founders, it’s elemental to making sure that fandom continues to grow. Fics are tagged based on original IP, relationships, tropes, and content warnings like “Dead Dove: Do Not Eat” that try to warn users that what they might be reading might not be for them.

All this means that the site has grown significantly, becoming a safe space for both creatives and readers to access stories that normally would not be available anywhere else. And it also means that if anything were to go awry, it can come with some serious consequences.

For authors, the Google Drive initiative is seen as their work being reposted or repurposed for something that it was not initially intended for: private consumption. Fans have always been able to download their favorite fanfics from AO3, but only so they can read them offline. It was never meant to be shared around other than the platforms where the author has uploaded the stories themself.

For some readers, however, it’s all about continuing the legacy of AO3 in the time that it’s down.

Authors, however, have called out the behavior: They never gave permission for the work to be uploaded anywhere else and they are concerned with Google Drive’s use of AI, which may scan their work for other uses.

“Sorry folks but I don’t give permission for my Ao3 fics to be shared on a Google Drive or uploaded to literally any other site,” one author wrote on Threads. “I know this is just me shouting into the void but it sure as hell would be nice if people didn’t give AI scrapers easy access to people’s writing.”

Unlike Google and the other sites like Wattpad and Tumblr, AO3 does not have to adhere to certain content guidelines as they are not-for-profit and do not run any ads on their site. AO3 is run by volunteers (hence the longer-than-normal downtime after this security incident) and through donations by paid members. As they are not making a profit on sex content or copyrighted intellectual properties, the site is able to protect itself under fair use.

For some fans, it feels like they’re just sharing the wealth in a time of uncertainty. But there’s no denying that this entire situation has become a bit of a slippery slope, both ethically and legally. Is community more important than that? That feels like an argument that we’re all constantly questioning nowadays even outside of fandom.

One thing is for sure: There’s no denying the impact that AO3 has had on fandom and its ability to be able to create works openly and without worry no matter the content. With it, the community has been able to grow safely and produce a number of works that have even gone on to live other lives through other means. Without it, there’s a chance that our time creating original stories with existing characters might be at risk yet again. The question is: How much are fans willing to bet for two to four weeks of comfort? It looks like they’re going all in, and it’ll be interesting to see who is holding the cards at the end.

#6883. Get Ready for the Holiday Season With These Timeless, Customizable Cards

Minted makes it easy to send personalized cheer to everyone you love.

Last year I spent a long weekend with a friend I hadn’t seen in a decade. When I got home, I sent her the usual ebullient text about how wonderful it was to see her again at last and how much the time meant to me. Oddly, she didn’t reply; I thought she’d ghosted me.

About two weeks later, I dragged myself begrudgingly to my mailbox to collect the usual bunch of mailers, bills, credit card offers, and insurance solicitations. I’d planned to stop by the recycle bucket and toss the whole stack. Then I saw something amid the circulars: a thick, silvery envelope, addressed with lavishly scrawled panache, stamped with sealing wax and postmarked with a limited edition portrait postage stamp featuring a favorite poet. The return address showed it came from my friend.

I was over the moon. Amid all the usual generic garbage, here was a gem—something written just for me. I rushed into the hall, found an actual letter opener in a drawer, and mindfully—lovingly—slit the envelope open to find an artist-designed card by Minted. Inside was a loving note from my friend, far more profound and personal than my erasable text to her. This card was something almost sacred that I could touch, feel, save, and read over and over again.

In a digital age of AI generated missives, disappearing chats, and emails nobody takes the time to read more than the first two sentences of, a letter or note in the form of a greeting card is an anomaly. Even holiday cards have, in many cases, gone digital, crammed between promotional emails and work obligations in our inboxes, blasé versions of their former snazzy, personalized selves. But aren’t we all craving more? Something we can treasure? Don’t we hanker to send something to friends and loved ones as special as the festive season itself?

Enter Minted, a clever stationery and design brand with a mission to use the designs of independent artists and elevated printing techniques—such as letterpress and foil—to bring back the art of card sending and letter writing. Their high end products provide first class alternatives that infuse any dispatch with gravitas, making communicating a mindful act. Bring on the sealing wax and calligraphy pens!

These stunning cards make a statement anytime of the year—but especially over the holidays. Their soul-felt artistry embodies the Japanese concept of geidō: the way of the arts, where every action is meant to be a ceremonial—artistic—celebration of life. Minted’s 2026 array of holiday options embrace heritage motifs, vintage details, vibrant colours, and candid photography. Minted Concierge offers complimentary one-on-one guidance to help you find holiday cards that fit your style and message, then personalize every detail—from the layout and photo to envelopes, liners, and stamps.

For a more keepsake-worthy finish, Photo Mount™ pairs a semi-gloss photo print, mounted by hand on thick premium cardstock, with a choice of foil, letterpress, or standard printing; Minted’s letterpress option gives soft cotton paper a deep, tactile impression, with ink hand-mixed in small batches and each piece reviewed for color and texture. New this year, both Peel & Seal and Mail It For Me make sending holiday cards easier. For larger mailings, Peel & Seal envelopes make sending simpler: a concealed adhesive strip seals with a press, no moisture needed, and select envelope colors are available. On select eligible card formats, Mail It For Me can handle printing, addressing, assembly, postage, and mailing through USPS for an additional fee. Minted confirms when the cards are handed off and will ship you any extra unaddressed cards and envelopes for last-minute list adds.

Greeting cards weren’t always computer generated, overly edited, and filtered to death; they’ve evolved over two centuries. While the first holiday greeting card was introduced in the UK in 1843 as a festive, shorthand option for Christmas letter writers who lacked the time to pen several missives at once, they were popularized during the Victorian era. For decades afterwards, decorative cards trended, each an elaborate work of art. As printing and personal photography became more sophisticated and widespread, so did the cards. Sending and receiving holiday cards was a de rigueur tradition, their appearance evoking the styles of each decade. But in digital times, greeting cards began to lose favor, often replaced (poorly!) by a quick email or text. The pandemic brought a resurgence—an indication that people sought to connect in old school ways—and new generations have taken a renewed interest in sending unique holiday cards.

Designed by independent artists, more relevant than ever, Minted’s custom cards bring the age-old tradition into the future. Combining modern tools and the human touch, Minted ensures that sending holiday cards personalizes the moment, with artistry and emotion that make opening every card feel like unwrapping a meaningful gift. Offering cards with contemporary flourishes as well as those that are imbued with vintage vibes, Minted’s options allow the senders to express themselves and set a mood.

Consider Starry Wonder by Alston Foster, one of 2026’s most celestial designs. It boasts a retro-inspired border made of glittery stars, designed to frame a favorite photo.

Whimsical Cut Paper Party by Inkblot Design pairs fluid, colorful shapes with a look that feels both sophisticated and playful. Fully customizable, the artist-designed card is available in myriad shapes and formats.

Photomatic by Ani Somi delves into the photo booth realm, presenting a perfect place to display your silliness and party spirit. The foil-pressed accents gleam, catching the light like tinsel on a Christmas tree.

Choose Modjeska by chocomocacino to connect with the past, perhaps your own childhood memories of the season. From the atelier of an independent artist, this design comes in a range of shapes and formats, each crafted to help tell your story.

Customize your cards with distinctive fonts and greetings, plus colorful backers or envelope liners, to make them uniquely yours. With dots that call to mind holiday lights aglow, Delicate Dots by Ekko Studio pairs shimmering colors with foil-pressed accents for a celebratory, bespoke card.

In the same way you might enjoy meticulously wrapping a gift, baking a holiday dish, or trimming a tree, taking the extra time to send a meaningful greeting to families and friends may be as much a gift to yourself as it is to those who receive your brilliant Minted-made card. Minted makes it easy to celebrate the season and foster connection.

#6882. In Case You Missed It, Everyone Is Wearing Novelty Jackets Right Now

The season doesn’t last forever—get yours before it’s too late.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

We’re in the Goldilocks of jacket season. It’s been a consistent 55 to 60 degrees (knock on wood)—a temperature that doesn’t subject you to puffers and parkas, but rather transitional outerwear. And our favorite piece in this category is the novelty jacket.

Frog fastening is an immediate novelty jacket tell. Zara has a sueded style featuring the type of closure in a contrasting design. Just under $80, it’s an accessible entry point to the trend if you want to test the waters.

We’ve been seeing renditions of Conner Ives’s Demi robe topping the most discerning street style looks, and Free People’s faux fur-collared jacket is one of our favorites.

Is it a sweater? Is it a jacket? Does it matter? Iro’s marled knit is a mid-weight layer that polishes off any look.

The sisters behind DannijoPro realized that women were really lacking cute, trend-forward pieces to wear to support their favorite teams, and they decided to do something about it. This sheeny bomber is the result of that decision—and there’s a whole other host of options if they don’t have your team’s colors available in this specific piece.

Yves Salomon’s jacket brings the drama with its voluminous fur number. Pare it down with light-washed jeans or use it to add the glamour to an LBD. It does it all.

The novelty jacket that’s just begging to be taken out to a fancy dinner and then a sultry soirée. Comprised of a luxe brocade, it’s rich, luxurious, and delivering an after-dark elegance.
