#6871. F1 Academy Driver Emma Felbermayr and Audi Reveal the Ultimate Way to Experience Race Weekend in Austin

In their first-ever weekend at the United States Grand Prix, the latest F1 team to join the grid celebrated in style.

Before Miami or Las Vegas, the United States Grand Prix in Austin, Texas, was the only race in the U.S. for Formula 1. And it has quite the history! The iconic Circuit of the Americas track was purposefully built for the sport and has become a quick favorite amongst drivers and fans alike thanks to its stunning turns that were inspired by the likes of the Silverstone Circuit in the United Kingdom and Bahrain’s International Circuit and spacing that allows for fun overturns and racing to happen at all parts of the circuit. (Try overtaking during a street race, we dare you!) But as a person who has experienced several races in her lifetime, the vibes of Austin stood out to me as the iconic venue brought the sport back to the Lone Star State once again for another incredible race that sets itself apart by being clear that it’s made by the fans and for the fans. And the mood was only elevated by the introduction of some brand-new teams in the circuit including the Audi Revolut F1 Team.

In a surprise twist, this year they were also joined by F1 Academy after a schedule change moved one of their planned three-race weekends over to Austin. Which also meant that for the first time, second-year driver Emma Felbermayr would be experiencing the track for herself. And as someone who was heading to the United States Grand Prix weekend for the first time alongside Audi, it felt like fate that we were going to have this new adventure together!

To get the epic Audi experience, the team invited us out to the 2026 United States Grand Prix to see race weekend up close. And in true Texas fashion, we got to see firsthand the real meaning of Southern hospitality. Get ready to find out how Audi is not only bringing a new kind of venture to Austin, but also how Emma got ready for an unexpected weekend in the Lone Star State.

(P.S. The writer received free stays, meals, and experiences in order to write this story.)

I was determined to finally get to witness an entire full race weekend for the first time (third time is the charm, after all), so I traveled out the day before so I wouldn’t have to miss a single second. Ironically, this has also been part of Emma’s plan for success this year.

“This year I took myself a bit more time to see the cities. Last year I was still in school. I always arrived the day before in the evening and went back on Sunday,” she revealed to Cosmopolitan after her final race of the weekend. “But this year, I arrive one day earlier before media day starts to do things you have to do in that specific city.”

Emma used the extra time to her advantage, saying that she went to the city to “try cowboy boots, hats, and all that stuff. But also the most important is getting used to the time zone.”

In a special surprise, Audi allowed me to bring a +1 and so I brought my dad out for his first official race. We hadn’t even made it to the track yet when he let out his first, “Oh my god,” as we were greeted by an Audi representative and were shuffled into a brand-new 2027 Audi Q9, our vehicle for the weekend. Our driver, Cody, let us know that the vehicle only had 18 miles on it before he picked it up, meaning we were truly one of the first to step into it.

Unfortunately, since we got in a bit late, we couldn’t really follow in Emma’s footsteps and take in the sights. Instead, our only stop after our flight was our hotel, the newly opened 1 Hotel Austin. The hotel has a strong link to Audi (more on that later) and we automatically felt right at home as we walked in and immediately headed up to our room.

Room service was absolutely necessary and I totally recommend their wagyu burger which also came with some perfectly cooked fries. After a quick shower, we settled in for the night as we got ready for an incredibly fun-filled weekend.

Practice day meant a more chill day at COTA and a later start than the following few days, so I was able to kick things off with a massage at the Bamford Wellness Spa in 1 Hotel Austin. Not only is there a sauna that I wish I had more time to enjoy, but the staff there were one in a million and made me feel so welcomed and relaxed. The deep tissue massage was a welcome reset after a flight and it got me so energized for an exciting first day.

Unlike in Formula 1, where drivers get three official practices before the qualifying session, Formula 1 Academy drivers already come into day one ready to participate in their first of three races of the weekend. While Emma notes that simulator training does help them be fully prepared for packed weekends like this one, nothing relates to the real thing.

“The track time you get isn't a lot. In karting you don't really learn that you have to prepare yourself that much for a weekend, because you've got five test sessions before every weekend. But here you only have one, so that's the only session you can try something on track and just go through different situations before a weekend,” Emma said.

And so, it was important for us to get the lay of the land. As my dad and I got to the track in the middle of the day and it was officially time to take everything in. We did a hot girl walk around the track going from Turn 1—where he would be taking in the race all weekend—before walking backwards the track’s direction until we hit turn 16. It allowed us to get a pretty good idea of what the track was like and also check out some of the incredible spots where other folks who were attending were able to see the races.

It was a shorter day at COTA, but for a good reason. We ran back to the hotel after doing some very important shopping at the F1 merch shops and got in just in time for our special dinner experience, “Driven by the Journey,” a special partnership between Audi, 1 Hotels, and SingleThread—a three-star Michelin restaurant located in Sonoma County in California.

The dinner brought the iconic SingleThread Farms experience to Austin with a special five-course menu created by owner-chef Kyle Connaughton that featured fresh produce picked from the farm and picked out by owner-farmer Katina Connaughton. Each course also came with a wine pairing and it was truly one of the most incredible dining journeys I’ve ever had the pleasure to try. And the views from the hotel’s 14th floor were absolutely gorgeous, especially as we caught the Austin sunset.

As the sun went down, we went straight up to the rooftop to Watr—1 Hotel’s Japanese-inspired restaurant—where the night was just getting started for the ultimate nightcap.

It was a very full day on the track, so we headed out first thing in the morning in order to catch every single minute. Somehow, Audi worked their magic and gave my dad the greatest gifts you can give an F1 fan: a Paddock Club pass. Some lucky fans are able to win the chance to go into the Paddock Club with a team, while a few tickets are also sold to the public, but usually it’s access that’s reserved for those who are close to the team like sponsors or friends and family.

After catching up with the rest of the group, we walked over to the pit lane to get a private tour of the Audi F1 garage. Not only did we get to see the cars up close, but we also got to see the incredible mechanics and engineers get ready for qualifying day.

We also caught Emma’s Q&A back in the Paddock Club, where she shared her experience so far in Austin and her hope for the rest of the weekend as she prepared for her second race.

“The track is so wide, so big, and with our cars being that small compared to F1 cars, it it's even bigger. So I'm enjoying it a lot,” she revealed.

Soon after, we were heading out to the track for the ultimate experience: a Pirelli Hot Lap. Since I was the first one up for our session, I ran straight into the special Audi RS 5 where Audi F1 ambassador and current F1 Academy mentor Carrie Schreiner took me for the ultimate ride around the track that went up to 150 miles per hour. Just when I thought that walking around COTA was the best experience, there’s nothing like actually getting driven around the track.

Then came the day’s biggest main events: the F1 qualifying race as well as F1 Academy’s Reverse Grid Race. Grabbing one of the team radios only available in the Paddock Club and in the garage, we were able to listen to the team’s communications that made for a fun and different experience compared to watching at home.

For dinner, we headed out into the town and checked out Comedor, a spectacular Mexican restaurant located in downtown Austin where we grabbed some guacamole topped with chapulines—or Mexican crickets—and some Crab Luis Totopos that I will be dreaming about for years.

Then it was back to 1 Hotel for a much-needed rest before the final day. Emma notes that relaxing and getting a good night’s sleep before a race day is always key.

“I like to get my nine hours every day. I just like getting into bed early and maybe watch something on Netflix, not doing so much,” she said. What’s she watching right now? Outer Banks and some reality TV, along with Gossip Girl as she prepares for her first trip to NYC with Formula 1 Academy. But no time for an all-nighter! There’s still race day to look forward to!

It was finally race day and things couldn’t be more heated as both Formula 1 and Formula 1 Academy geared up for another wild day.

After grabbing a smoothie at Neighbors Cafe at 1 Hotel, my dad and I got one final look around the track.

Bonus: There’s one new experience at the United States Grand Prix that blew my mind during race day: the Guest House at Turn 20. In partnership with Austin’s Guest House restaurant, the special private hospitality space gives the perfect view of the final turn into the pit lane and includes a gourmet menu, fun performances throughout the day, and a bar with specialty drinks. Believe me, if you want to get as close to the Paddock Club experience, this is it!

We got to the track just in time to see Emma in her final race of the weekend which left her third in the overall Formula 1 Academy standings. There’s only one more race left in the F1A calendar: Las Vegas.

“It's going to be so much colder, so it's going to be different. Since I've raced there already last year, that's at least an advantage for myself compared to this weekend,” she added. “I think I just can focus a bit more on the driving through practice than learning the track.”

Emma is looking forward to resetting herself before then, something she says is key to further success.

“Either after a bad or a good weekend, you need to reset. If it was a good weekend, it doesn't mean the next one is going to be good as well. But if it's a bad one, there is a chance to come back stronger.”

Part of that includes hanging out with friends and family while she’s back home.

“I love seeing my friends. Since we are traveling so much, I don't get to see them really often. But when I do, it's even more nice,” Emma revealed.

She hasn’t revealed her plans for next season, especially as she finishes up her two-year limit in Formula 1 Academy.

“I'd say it's a bit hard to say now. I don't have proper plans for next year, so we are a bit on seeing the options,” she noted.

But, above all, she’s keeping one thing in mind: her love of racing.

“It was always about having fun, enjoying it, and that's also why I do it. I've always done what I really enjoyed doing and what I felt was the right thing to do.”

And so, with one final look at COTA and a last “yee-haw,” we officially said goodbye to the United States Grand Prix and Audi’s first rodeo in Austin. If this is what their first go-around is like, we can’t wait to witness what they have coming up next!

#6864. Jennifer Lopez’s ‘Frosted Pearl’ Nails Are This Season’s Dreamiest Manicure Trend: How to Try Them Now

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Between fashion week appearances and making a return to music, Jennifer Lopez has become a major beauty muse for fall 2026. A few weeks ago, her cozy, “chai latte” nails—courtesy of her longtime manicurist Tom Bachik—served as our top inspiration for new-season manicures. Now? The duo has added “frosted pearl” to the list.

In an Instagram post yesterday, Lopez sported a sheer base with a soft, luminous sheen; the result felt like a fresh take on classic chrome. According to pro nail artist Jessica White, the new trend features a reflective “glaze”—which Hailey Bieber famously popularized—but in a subtler way. “Rather than an intense, mirror-like chrome, it catches the light gently, giving the nails a pearlescent finish.”

Lopez’s natural blush-toned base color is essential to the frosted pearl effect, says White. “A sheer milky white or soft nude creates a delicate pearl effect, whereas a pinker base gives a warmer finish.”

With the colder seasons in full swing, understated, minimalist nail trends are to be expected; those who typically refrain from bold colors and elaborate nail art will often turn to traditional nudes, creamy neutrals, and your-nails-but-better manicures.

But White considers frosted pearl to be the best modern alternative. “There’s something about the subtle shimmer that feels a little more elevated and luxurious for the coming months,” she notes.

“For minimalists who want to introduce something a little different, it still feels clean, understated, and incredibly wearable, just with an added touch of luminosity.”

For the pearl finish, White explains that “chrome powder is the professional technique, but you can find chrome-like nail polishes, which are perfect for at-home.” Opt for one with a pink base. Sealing everything with a quality top coat will extend the life of your manicure and enhance the glossy shine.

#6859. The Tragic Story Behind La Bola Negra, One of the Year’s Best Films

Based on an unfinished play by Federico Garcia Lorca, the movie is already garnering Oscar buzz

It is rare to come across uncompromisingly queer work from LGBTQ+ artists from a century ago and beyond. Many writers did not get to see their work published in their own lifetime. E.M. Forster’s groundbreaking novel Maurice, which he began writing in 1913 and featured a rare-for-the-time happy ending, was not published until a year after his death, in 1971; Emily Dickinson’s romantic stanzas, which many critics believe were dedicated to her sister-in-law, Susan Gilbert Dickinson, weren’t even discovered until long after her passing. But even those queer artists who did see their work out in the world didn’t manage so easily. James Baldwin was told by his agent to burn the manuscript for Giovanni’s Room. Jane Rule was rejected more than 20 times in her efforts to find a publisher for Desert of the Heart.

One of this year’s most critically acclaimed films, La Bola Negra (“the black ball” in English), is based on an unfinished play of the same name written by the Spanish poet and playwright Federico Garcia Lorca in 1936. In the four pages of the story that remain, a wealthy young man is trying to gain entrance to an exclusive casino in Granada. In order to determine his admittance, the members turn to a voting system where they choose to roll a white ball in favor or a black ball in opposition. After they vote, he is handed a black ball; later, when his father asks him why he thinks he was rejected, the boy tells him that it’s because he is gay.

Tragically, Lorca never got the chance to finish what may have been a groundbreaking story. (He was shot dead by nationalist forces after being imprisoned during the beginning of the Spanish Civil War, and his remains have never been found.) But he had detailed his ideas for the drama in a letter to fellow playwright Cipriano Rivas Cherif.

That’s where Javier Calvo and Javier Ambrossi, the directing duo endearingly referred to as “Los Javis,” comes in. Their movie rewrites a bit of history, making it so that Lorca (also a character in the film) manages to complete most of his play, save for the last few pages. The film jumps between three different years—1932, 1937, and 2017—to explore the lives of different gay men across a century who are all loosely connected by the writer’s work. (The cast includes stars like Élite’s Miguel Bernardeau, folk-pop singer Guitarricadelafuente, and Oscar nominee Glenn Close.)

“We don’t have that many movies or books about the history that connects us as a community, the queer community,” co-director Ambrossi told NPR earlier this week. “That’s why we wanted to make it big. We wanted to make it epic, and we wanted to make it bold.”

To try to piece together the story, the directors combed through old letters and archives to try to imagine how Lorca might have ended it. “We were like detectives,” co-director Calvo noted. “It was fun to discover that he would like to talk about his projects in parties.” And these pieces are precisely how the story of La Bola Negra came together (but don’t worry, no spoilers here).

Now, Los Javis are bringing light to this untold story in an effort to preserve queer history and maintain the legacy of someone who couldn’t so freely tell his truth.

“We are all connected by this black ball that we all share, that we pass to each other, every generation—a black ball that is silence, pain, shame, but also it’s a spark of life and desire and love and joy,” Calvo said. “I feel like [this project] made me feel proud of my community and of the brave people that fought before me.”

You can watch La Bola Negra in theaters now. It arrives to Netflix on December 2.

#6857. Maura Higgins Seemingly Responds to Harsh Fan Feedback After Her “Toughest” Week on ‘DWTS’

“Some weeks will be better than others, and that’s okay. I’m only human,” she wrote, in part.

Maura Higgins is opening up after tackling her Disney Night choreography as Tinker Bell on Dancing With the Stars. The Love Island alum got candid in a lengthy statement she penned alongside a carousel of behind-the-scenes photos from Monday night’s episode after fans criticized her performance in Team Slaytopia’s group number.

“This week was definitely one of the toughest I’ve had so far. I was really unwell, running on very little energy and trying to learn two dances, but I gave it absolutely everything I had,” she started her message, adding that she had no previous dance experience before joining DWTS.“I wanted to challenge myself, step outside my comfort zone and give something completely new my absolute best & that’s exactly what I’m trying to do every single week. I’m learning, I’m making mistakes, I’m pushing myself and I’m growing along the way!! Some weeks will be better than others, and that’s okay,” she continued, adding, “I’m only human.”She insisted she wasn’t sharing the message for “sympathy or to make excuses, but simply to share a little of the reality behind the scenes.”

The Traitors star uplifted her pro partner and “biggest support throughout this journey,” Mark Ballas, before seemingly addressing harsh feedback from viewers who criticized her “low energy” performances in both of her numbers this week.

“Genuinely what is up with maura tonight…she was the clear weak link of that team dance,” one person wrote, while another added, “i'm going to be very honest with you i genuinely think maura did by far the worst of any celebrity tonight and the judges were just distracted by how good of a show mark is able to put on that they didn't notice lol.”

Per her message, Maura seems to be focusing on those who continue to uplift and support her throughout the competition.

“To everyone who continues to support me, encourage me and cheer me on, please know how much it means,” she wrote toward the end of her statement. “You genuinely give me the strength to keep going, especially on the harder days. Your kindness never goes unnoticed.”

She then closed her post by expressing how “grateful” she is to still be in the running for the iconic Mirrorball trophy.

Based on that alone, I tink she’ll be just fine either way!

#6856. 8 Takeaways from Design Miami and Art Basel in Paris

Inside the week’s standout moments, from Miu Miu to Madonna.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE Decor editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

There was plenty of ground to cover in Paris last week where the French editions of Design Miami and Art Basel took place alongside a full slate of noteworthy openings and events. (“I have 55 invites this week,” said one harried interiors maven in between stops.) Though the protests in Paris had temporarily subsided by the time the fairs began, they remained top of mind. But still, there was a sense of optimism in the air, which could be chalked up to the unifying power of art and design.

This was the fourth consecutive year that Design Miami, Paris was shown at L’Hôtel de Maisons (the one-time home of Karl Lagerfeld), and the second year that it has been led by Jesse Lee, the proprietor of the luxury resale platform Basic. Space. Below, a few of the best moments from the week.

Jeanne Greenberg Rohatyn’s Salon 94 Design received the award for Best Gallery Presentation at Design Miami Paris. The New York gallerist took over the far end of the first floor and filled it with an eclectic mix of pieces: Next to chairs by Alberto Giacometti, she showed furniture—sleek “Stag Stools” replete with protruding antlers—by Rick Owens and Michèle Lamy for the first time. Celebrated artist (and occasional Acne model) Peter Schlesinger quietly stood by as guests admired his large ceramic swan and geese urns, which were fired in a kiln in the garage of his Long Island home. Overlooking it all was a sculptural chandelier by Rome-based designer F. Taylor Colantonio that looked as if it was sliced from the surface of the moon and embedded with gemstones.

For Apple’s second annual Designers of Tomorrow showcase, a quartet of creators were issued a singular prompt: presence, or rather how to hold people’s attention amid the hurried pace of the modern world. The Brooklyn-based artist Isabel Rower created “Padded Chair, 1860,” which was based on a deep dive into 18th- and 19th-century paper-mâché furniture. Fifteen hundred handmade beads lined the bottom of the piece causing more than one showgoer to comment on the work’s ASMR potential. Other participants included Salem S. Charabi, Yoonjeong Lee, and Genki Matsumura and Sebastián Zorrilla of Parafernalia. “It was easy to identify these four designers and give them this platform,” said Ago Interiors founder Rodman Primack who served as the curator of the project.

At Galerie Anne-Sophie Duval in nearby Saint-Germain-des-Prés, the renowned French architect and designer Sylvain Dubuisson presented a series of ogle-worthy pieces: a sleek black desk shellacked with Japanese lacquer, a gold table with metal legs that was designed to oscillate “just for fun,” and another wooden table that was held together vertically by a peg at its center. When the peg was released, the table went horizontal and became a functional dining piece. Another long marble table could be folded into thirds. Even though she no longer runs the gallery (that honor has been bestowed on her daughter, Julie Blum), Duval was on hand on Thursday night to show off Dubuisson’s playful and imaginative inventions.

The new showroom of ELLE Decor Rising Star Hugo Besnier is an enchanting reminder of all that Paris has to offer. Located in a historic loft off a courtyard in the heart of Saint-Germain-des-Prés, the pedigree of previous inhabitants includes Balthus, Sheila Hicks, and even Vanessa Paradis—David Hockney was once a neighbor. In redesigning the property, Besnier took care to include works of these celebrated artists on the walls. The real function of the space is to not only show off his skills as an interior designer, but also his new collection of furniture, Tour de Mains, which works perfectly in the stylized surroundings.

Shattered prismatic forms by Alteronce Gumby at Jeffrey Deitch and new woven wall hangings by Sheila Hicks at Sikkema Malloy Jenkins were among our favorite pieces that bridged both art and objets. On the Palais’s second level, we encountered Marina Faust’s light sculptures, essentially stripped-down chandeliers hanging from a metal frame. Faust, a former photographer in her seventies, partnered with J. & L. Lobmeyr on these works. They were shown at the booth of Vienna-based gallery Gianni Manhattan.

At Hauser & Wirth Paris, a very specific collection pinpointed a certain passage in Alexander Calder’s life. Curated by Sandy Rower, the artist’s grandson and the founder and president of the Calder Foundation, Calder: François Premier is an examination of 1953–54 when the artist returned to live in the French countryside and had a very productive year both in terms of creating mobiles—Les Granettes (1953), and The Putney Mobile (1954)—trippy ink and gouache paintings, and household bric-a-brac like a homemade metal tea strainer.

French filmmaker Alice Diop, in her second collaboration with Miu Miu, presented Geste de Consolation at the Palais d’Iéna where Miu Miu stages its bi-annual runway shows. The project was the director’s first foray into making an exhibition, and the sampling of works packed a powerful punch. The four interconnected vignettes included a powerful 11-minute video directed by Diop; a purple-hued library and living room space with a curation of Diop’s favorite books and authors such as Chantal Akerman and Christina Sharpe; an ode to an African deity in Simone Leigh’s 16-foot-tall sculpture, Sentinel (Mami Wata); and a poignant photograph by Zanele Muholi. Just beyond the library in the makeshift auditorium, Ivorian choreographer Nadia Beugré arranged for live performances, while concerts were conducted by Angel Bat Dawid and the Sistazz of the Nitty Gritty.

The week even yielded a surprise appearance by Madonna, who was in town to celebrate her son Rocco Ritchie’s new exhibition Back on the Brush—a collection of 11 figurative expressionist paintings and sculptures that were shown at Pierre Augustin Rose. On view until November 7, the richly textured works are “rooted in observation, memory, and lived experience.” Says mom: “I love what he is doing!”

Sarah Cristobal is a writer, editor, and creative consultant. She is currently editor-at-large for 10 Magazine USA, among other freelance projects.

#6854. Beyoncé Shares a Rare Look at Blue Ivy Carter’s Casual Style in a New Family Photo

Earlier this month, Blue Ivy Carter joined her father, Jay-Z, during night one of his Jay-Z 30 Tour in Los Angeles, playing piano for “The Story of O.J.” and “Dead Presidents.” While the 14-year-old didn’t take the stage for night two, she was still at the show, supporting him.

Blue wanted it for herself. She took it seriously and she earned it. And most importantly, she had fun! We all watched her grow more and more every night before our eyes.”

#6851. These 5 Brands Are Making the Holiday Pajama Sets I Want Most—I've Rounded Up the Best Ones

I'd happily make these my everyday uniform if I could!!

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Sure, decorating my house, lighting a fresh pine-scented candle, and listening to Christmas music all day, every day get me in the holiday spirit, but what really does it for me? A pair of matching holiday pajamas. As someone who wears a two-piece set to bed pretty much every single night, I love swapping in new prints and colors to fit the time of year. There’s just something about a festive set that really completes the cozy vibe!

That said, I’m not the type of person who’ll settle for a basic plaid set and call it a day. With brands dropping holiday PJs in everything from cool patterns to out-of-the-box color combos, you'll find plenty of unique options to choose from. Ahead, I rounded up five brands with seriously cute designs that'll give your nighttime wardrobe a seasonal refresh. They also make great gifts for loved ones or the perfect picks for your next matching-pajama party with friends and family!

For as long as I can remember, Old Navy has been one of the go-tos for holiday pajama sets. The brand always comes through with cute designs at affordable prices—something that feels increasingly rare in this economy! Its iconic flannel sets come in all kinds of styles, from classic plaid and gingerbread prints to lively options featuring elves, stripes, and mistletoe. Even better? Tons of this season’s styles are currently 60% off, so I’d run, not walk!

Known for its fun, quirky designs, Roller Rabbit has perfected the art of making PJs that feel anything but basic (the monkeys!) while still being incredibly cozy. Its holiday collection is no exception, with colorful prints, funky patterns, and plenty of personality. If you're a maximalist or you just love a bold pajama set, this is where to get them! I know they’re on the pricier side, but I promise they’re worth the splurge! (Ahem, these tend to sell out pretty quickly, so I’d add them to your cart ASAP if you have your eye on a pair!)

You know and love SKIMS for its buttery-soft loungewear and impressive shapewear, and I’m happy to report that its pajama selection is just as good. Right now, the holiday sets are especially gorgeous! I love how the brand sticks to the classics with familiar silhouettes, like cami sets, while giving them a festive update. Personally, I’ll be buying the bright pink plaid set!

From my teenage years to now, Victoria's Secret has always been at the top of my list for bras, underwear, loungewear, and, of course, incredibly cute pajama sets. I remember feeling so grown-up whenever I'd get a new pair from the brand, and honestly, that hasn't changed! Its 2026 holiday collection is just as good as ever, with on-trend designs that feel a little more dressed-up than your everyday PJs. Choose from cute short sets and long flannel styles for those who love something extra cozy, to silky satin sets and lightweight Henley styles for the girls who tend to run hot. There's something for every kind of sleeper!

When I think of the silkiest, most luxe pajamas I’ve ever worn, Eberjey instantly comes to mind. They’re exactly what you’d imagine sleeping in a five-star hotel would feel like: silky to the touch, featherlight, and soft as a cloud. And the holiday styles are just as dreamy! Available in the brand’s beloved TENCEL™ Modal and flannel sets, there’s a range of festive yet chic designs to choose from, including polka dots, bows, holly berries, and more.

#6849. How to Choose a Graphic Print Bag That Won’t Go Out of Style

Exploring the power of pattern through the lens of Dior’s Cigale.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

A printed accessory can bring color, dimension, and personality to an outfit without requiring much else. The key is finding a design that works with the clothes you already own, suits your daily needs, and feels relevant beyond a single season. Dior offers an interesting case study, particularly when it comes to houndstooth, a motif closely associated with the house’s history and Christian Dior’s use of menswear-inspired fabrics in women’s fashion. The Dior Cigale bag, Mini Lady Dior Bag, and Bow bag give us a starting point for exploring the appeal of graphic prints and what makes a patterned accessory a worthwhile long-term purchase.

A graphic print should have enough character to stand out, but it should also work with the clothes you already wear. Houndstooth has a strong geometric pattern that can look just as good with denim as it does with formal, more tailored styles. If you prefer something more playful, a colorful or embellished design can make a familiar outfit feel different.

The bag’s shape and construction matter, too. Consider its size, how you plan to carry it, and whether the materials suit your lifestyle. A bag you intend to use regularly should be practical enough for your routine, with craftsmanship that holds up to frequent wear. The Cigale, for example, has a sculptural silhouette inspired by the architectural lines of a 1952 Christian Dior gown. That connection to couture gives the design a distinct identity, while its silhouette offers something to consider beyond the print alone.

A graphic print bag can work with a range of outfits, particularly when its colors complement the rest of your wardrobe. A black-and-white houndstooth pattern, for instance, pairs naturally with neutral knitwear and sharp separates. More colorful prints can coordinate just as well, provided you give them room to stand out.

The simplest approach is to keep the rest of your outfit relatively restrained. A printed bag can add interest to a white shirt and trousers or break up an all-black look without requiring a complete wardrobe overhaul. If you prefer a more coordinated outfit, pick up one of the bag’s colors in your shoes or another accessory. You don’t need to match everything. Just a little repetition is enough to make the look feel deliberate.

No accessory is immune to changing tastes, but some patterns have been around longer than others. Houndstooth, for one, predates Dior, but the designer incorporated it into his collections alongside other fabrics traditionally associated with menswear. He used these materials in women’s suits and dresses, pairing their masculine character with silhouettes that emphasized the female form. The brand continues to revisit houndstooth, including on the Cigale bag.

If you’re buying a graphic print bag to carry for years, think about whether you’ll wear it with more than one or two outfits. A pattern that works with your everyday wardrobe will get more use than one that calls for a specific look. It’s also worth considering whether you like the bag’s shape on its own. Trends come and go, but a bag you enjoy carrying for reasons beyond its print has a better chance of staying in rotation.

Start with the clothes you already wear. A graphic print bag can add interest to everyday staples like straight-leg jeans, a simple knit, and loafers, or bring some personality to office staples like tailored trousers and a crisp shirt. For dinner, try one with a black dress or a streamlined suit. The idea is to let the print add something to your outfit without making getting dressed more complicated.

You can mix prints, too. A fine stripe or small check can work with a bolder graphic, especially when the colors complement each other. If the combination feels too busy, simplify the rest of the look. With the Cigale, clean lines and structured tailoring can play off its sculptural shape. And remember, the bag doesn’t need much competition. A few well-chosen pieces will let it stand out without overwhelming the outfit.

A graphic print can draw you to a bag, but the details are what make it worth the investment. Look closely at the stitching, hardware, lining, and finish. The materials should feel appropriate for the price, and the construction should hold up to regular use. Consider how the print is worked into the design, too. The pattern should feel like part of the bag, not an afterthought.

With Dior, the appeal also lies in how the house approaches familiar motifs. Its graphic patterns are part of a broader design vocabulary, reinterpreted through changing silhouettes, materials, and embellishments. That gives the Cigale context beyond its appearance alone. Ultimately, a bag’s value comes down to what it offers in design and execution, not just the name on the label.

#6847. The Blue Door House from Notting Hill Just Hit the Market

The fictional London home of Hugh Grant’s bashful bookseller is now available for $9.9 million.

It’s perhaps the most famous house in one of London’s most charming neighborhoods, so beloved that crowds are known to gather around it like the English version of Carrie Bradshaw’s West Village apartment. It’s the blue-doored townhouse where Hugh Grant’s fictional bookseller lived and wooed Julia Roberts’s paparazzi-plagued Hollywood actress in 1999’s Notting Hill.

Despite starring in a blockbuster rom-com, the 2,750-square-foot house in question is very much real—and now it can be yours. That is, for roughly $10 million.

Not coincidentally, the three-bedroom residence also once belonged to Richard Curtis, the screenwriter behind Notting Hill, which was directed by Roger Michell and went on to gross some $364 million worldwide.

Behind the recognizable front door—painted a glossy, rich shade of royal blue—the house unfolds with an entrance vestibule and courtyard before reaching a cavernous great room topped by a vaulted ceiling and a row of skylights. Nearly 50 feet in length, the soaring space was originally an artist’s studio and now does triple duty as a kitchen, dining, and reception area. The room has hardwood floors, a stone fireplace, deep-green cabinetry, and a marble-topped island. Two guest rooms can also be found elsewhere on the floor, each with its own private bath.

Upstairs, the primary suite comes with a dual-sink bathroom equipped with both a freestanding tub and a shower. This floor also features a lofted library and office that overlooks the ground level’s central room, plus a spiral staircase that leads to the roof terrace.

Other outdoor spaces include a small balcony off the second floor and an expansive ground-level courtyard. It might not come with a bumbling Hugh Grant, but the house certainly has charm to spare.

The Blue Door House is listed for £7.5 million with Josh Ratcliff of UK Sotheby’s International Realty.

Geoffrey Montes is an associate editor at ELLE Decor with a serious love for all things real estate and design. Before that, he worked at Architectural Digest, Galerie, and Preservation magazines, covering everything from jaw-dropping listings to world-famous architects and design events like Salone del Mobile and Homo Faber.

#6843. These 7 Storage Solutions for Small Spaces Look Too Good to Hide

Display-worthy baskets, glass-fronted cabinets, and colorful tins can make organization part of your decor.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

From a Brooklyn studio to a narrow Baltimore rowhouse, two decades of small-space living have taught me how to make every square foot count. Add in my Type A Virgo tendencies and an appreciation for good design, and I have strong opinions about storage: Namely, it should be as good-looking as it is hard-working. My first stop when hunting for products that deliver just that is always IKEA. The Swedish retailer has an unmatched selection of storage furniture and decor. Here are seven pieces that offer both function and form.

I have never known the pleasure of a linen closet, but I’ve gotten so many compliments on this metal-and-glass cabinet in my bathroom that I no longer mind. The reeded glass offers an unexpected texture while obscuring what’s inside to reduce visual clutter, and each interior shelf is roomy enough to hold a stack of towels, plus a small bin for toiletries and medicine bottles. I like to use the bamboo UPPDATERA boxes for material contrast; the larger style works great on the lower shelves to tame my mess of serums, makeup, lotions, and the like.

There is no such thing as too many bins or baskets when you live in a small space. They are the unsung organizational heroes, giving you a way to stash all the miscellaneous things that would typically be tucked away in a closet, attic, basement, or even a spare room in a larger home. IKEA sells a huge variety, but the woven styles like this one are particularly lovely to keep on display. I use them on my EKET living room shelves for games, in my bedroom for scarves and bags, and in the kitchen for tea towels and napkins.

When square footage is at a premium, closets tend to get eliminated. A free-standing wardrobe is the solution to creating clothes storage in a small bedroom. This chic new design features sliding doors—a space-saver itself since you won’t need to account for door clearance—adjustable shelves, and two drawers.

If your front door, like mine, opens directly into your living space, you need to fake a foyer as best you can. The key to that is shoe storage—otherwise, guests will be staring at muddy boots the minute they walk into your home. This cabinet hides 14 pairs behind a traditional design, and sticks out from the wall less than a foot. Two drawers make space for keys and sunglasses while four hooks give bags and dog leashes a home.

All kitchens can benefit from more storage, but it’s the small cook spaces where you really need to get creative. A mobile cart offers that utility, and this one features three shelves for anything from spices and baking supplies to serveware or a coffee bar.

Another simple small kitchen storage idea: Keep your dry goods in stackable containers, like the BETTHAJ tins, to maximize your pantry shelf space. They’re far easier to handle than flimsy paper containers or plastic bags, plus, they look great on a countertop or open shelving. They come in five different sizes, from 19 oz. to 3.3 qt., so you can mix and match to suit your needs.

Why choose between extra seating or more storage when you can have both? This sturdy little pouf hides a compartment that I use for an extra throw blanket, but it could also fit a few games, your knitting supplies, or remote controls. The cushioned top is taut enough that it can also function as a side table, making this a small space triple threat.

Head to IKEA to find storage that maximizes space without sacrificing style.

#6841. What Does Non-Acnegenic Mean, Exactly? Dermatologists Break Down the Skincare Term

And I thought I knew all the acne buzzwords...

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

When I was a young girl in the early 2000s, there was always a bottle of that iconic blue Cetaphil Gentle Skin Cleanser in my parents’ house. It was probably the only thing I washed my face with for the first 10 years of my life. But what stuck with me the longest (and perhaps was an early sign that I was destined to be a beauty editor) was that, right under the product name, the label said “non-comedogenic.” This new word perplexed me. I vividly remember trying to figure out how to pronounce it as I washed my face every evening, finally asking my mom during my very short nighttime skincare routine. She told me how to say it and what it meant in a way that a 7 year old would understand, and that was that. I was all up to speed.

Fast forward two decades later, and I’m sitting here, perplexed once again, as I learn about a new-to-me, related-to-non-comedogenic term: non-acnegenic. I’ve been seeing the word pop up here and there when it comes to an acne-friendly skincare routine, but I had to know, what actually makes it different from non-comedogenic? Is it a marketing buzzword, or is whether or not a product is non-acnegenic really important to pay attention to? I had far more questions than 7-year-old me, so I turned to two dermatologists to explain everything.

Let’s start with some good news: The definition of non-acnegenic is honestly pretty straightforward. “Non-acnegenic means a product has been formulated and tested so it won’t trigger acne breakouts, including the red and inflamed bumps and pustules most people picture when they think of acne,” says board-certified dermatologist Mona Foad, MD.

If you have acne-prone skin and/or are currently trying to figure out how to get rid of acne, Dr. Foad calls it “one of the most useful labels you can look for.” This is because brands “typically test these products on people with acne-prone skin over several weeks with a dermatologist evaluating the skin for any new breakouts,” she explains.

However, using a non-acnegenic skincare product isn’t a 100 percent guarantee that it won’t cause a breakout, unfortunately. “Think ‘less likely to invite a breakout,’” says board-certified dermatologist Mona Gohara, MD. This label means the product is unlikely to cause or worsen acne, but there’s no way for a brand to promise that result for everyone.

Non-acnegenic and non-comedogenic are different—but there’s plenty of overlap, Dr. Gohara says. Non-comedogenic, as you may already know, means that a product “is designed not to clog pores,” says Dr. Foad, who notes that a comedone is the technical term for a clogged pore. “Non-comedogenic addresses clogged pores, while non-acnegenic addresses the full picture of acne, including inflammation.”

Here’s what I found really interesting: “A product can avoid clogging pores and still irritate the skin enough to trigger inflammatory breakouts,” says Dr. Foad. Just because a product is non-comedogenic doesn’t mean you won’t get a breakout after using it. But that doesn’t mean using a non-comedogenic product can’t be helpful in avoiding acne, either. “Clogged pores are the starting point for acne, so preventing them is the first line of defense,” Dr. Foad explains. “When a product carries this label, the brand is claiming that its formula won’t block your pores and set the stage for breakouts.” Think of it this way: Acne-prone skin wants protection from potential pore cloggers and acne triggers alike. So, if you’re shopping for acne-prone skin, Dr. Foad recommends looking for products that are both non-comedogenic and non-acnegenic.

Oh, and another important thing to note: “Neither means the product actually treats acne,” says Dr. Gohara. Remember, these labels just mean that you’re putting skincare or makeup on your face that is less likely to set off a breakout.

As discussed above, non-comedogenic and non-acnegenic do have accepted definitions in the beauty industry. But there’s one super important caveat: Just like any non-medical claim on an over-the-counter beauty product, neither of these terms is regulated by the FDA. “This is one of the biggest sources of confusion I see,” says Dr. Foad. Because the FDA doesn’t define or regulate either of these, “there’s no standard test brands are required to use,” she says.

What does that mean, exactly? “One company might test a product on dozens of acne-prone volunteers over several weeks, while another might rely on ingredient lists alone,” explains Dr. Foad. “That’s why these labels are a helpful starting point, not a guarantee.” Your best bet is to look for brands that are transparent about their clinical and consumer testing, so you can review those and decide for yourself if a product looks promising enough to buy.

There’s no one magical ingredient that automatically makes a product non-acnegenic, but these are often used in products meant to support acne-prone skin:

Again, the inclusion of these ingredients doesn’t guarantee you won’t break out after using them. If you have an allergy or sensitivity to anything on this list, you should avoid it.

On the flip side, here are some ingredients to avoid, as they can trigger breakouts and clogged pores:

In general, for those who are acne-prone, “I’d steer them away from heavy, oil-based foundations, concealers, and facial balms,” says Dr. Gohara. Instead, look for lightweight foundations and other makeup products that don’t contain the ingredients above.

Of course, anyone who’s acne-prone, oily, or combination may want to ensure their beauty products are non-acnegenic. But beyond that, “they’re also essential for patients using prescription acne treatments like tretinoin or benzoyl peroxide, which can be drying and require a moisturizer that won’t undo their progress,” says Dr. Foad.

While pimples are often associated with teenagers, you may also benefit from non-acnegenic products as you age: “I also see many women dealing with hormonal acne in their 30s and 40s, and through perimenopause and menopause, when breakouts can appear for the first time in years,” she adds.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan with five years of experience researching and writing skincare and makeup stories.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.

#6836. Demi Moore Wears a Gown Made of Jeans to the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards

Zac Posen transformed classic denim waistbands into sculptural eveningwear.

There’s no denim without Demi. At tonight’s 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards in New York City, Demi Moore stepped out in a tongue-in-cheek GapStudio look that redefined the meaning of denim-on-denim.

Designed by Zac Posen, Moore’s custom gown centered on a series of indigo denim waistbands stitched one atop another to form a corseted shape. The bodice featured a halter neckline with folded-over ends, while the low-waisted maxi skirt had a long slit and a slight train.

Moore completed the look with suede pumps, diamond drop earrings, and, of course, a fully denim handbag created by the brand’s executive director of accessories, Reed Krakoff.

Later in the evening, the actress presented the Innovation Award to Richard Dickson and Zac Posen on behalf of Gap Inc. Dickson and Posen, naturally, both wore denim too.

While sculptural and evening-ready, Moore’s gown put a distinctly American spin on the evening’s “American Black Tie” theme. Plus, denim is woven into the fabric of American style—and Gap’s DNA, too.

“Denim is at the heart of Gap’s identity, and I wanted to explore its function in an entirely new way for my dear friend Demi,” Posen said in a statement. “By deconstructing the familiar details of a classic jean and reimagining them through the lens of elevated eveningwear, we created something sculptural, unexpected, and distinctly GapStudio.”

#6831. The Fall Denim Trend Giving Barrel Jeans a New Shape

These are the most flattering, effortless styles to shop now.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Cocooning proportions were rife on the fall/winter 2026 runways. From billowing dresses at Stella McCartney and Jil Sander to long, rounded sweater vests at Chanel and expressive, cloud-shaped trousers at Chloé, the message was clear: eye-catching silhouettes are in.

When it comes to denim, the cocoon silhouette thrives. Not quite as dramatic as barrel-leg styles and occasionally featuring gentle pleating, the subtle banana-inspired shape is made to flatter the female form. Sitting snugly on the hips and gently blossoming out, they taper only slightly at the bottoms, allowing plenty of space for the wearer to move freely.

Horseshoe-shaped jeans first gained popularity on Alaïa’s spring/summer 2025 runway for their ability to add interest to an otherwise minimalist look—and the cocoon silhouette moves the trend on, offering a subtle tweak to the runway-inspired style.

In terms of styling, they are ideal for autumn days. Throw them on with a V-neck cashmere knit and a pair of pumps, and what would have been a simple ensemble is transformed by the directional curvature of the denim. For evening, watch them do all the heavy lifting, turning a cami top and kitten heels into a dinner-ready outfit.

It comes as no surprise that Japanese high-street stalwart Muji, known for its refined homeware and wardrobe solutions, has created a perfect pair of cocoon jeans. The lightweight denim features a slightly curved leg shape and the kapok-blend fabric feels soft to wear, while keeping the structure of classic denim.

As for a slightly more substantial take, Topshop’s pair features a front pleat detail that helps to create a sculpted, dimensional shape through the leg. The oversized proportions and gently tapered ankle create an effortlessly cool look, while the mid-rise waist and oversized, sculptural leg give them an edge that will swiftly upgrade your outfits.

If you’re ready to level up your denim drawer, you really can’t go wrong with either pair. Shop them below.

#6829. The 15 Best Moisturizers Perfect for Mature Skin

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Nourishing favorites from brands like Augustinus Bader and ZIIP come expert-recommended

There are plenty of ways to treat signs of aging, but among the most effective is also the simplest: Striking the right moisture balance for your skin. Proper hydration can help reduce the appearance of fine lines and dullness, while also improving skin laxity over time. While almost any face cream can boost hydration, the best moisturizers for mature skin provide targeted, preventative treatments in a jar.

Key ingredients, such as peptides, retinoids, and others, work together to target specific concerns. “Retinoids or plant-based alternative bakuchiol improve and prevent fine lines, increase cellular turnover, improve skin texture and elasticity, and [reduce] discoloration,” dermatologist Morayo Adisa tells Bazaar. “Look for antioxidants such as vitamin C and E to help with collagen production and decrease oxidative stress. Hyaluronic acid, glycerin, and urea to increase skin hydration, softening the appearance of lines and wrinkles. Also, look for an emollient such as squalane or shea butter.”

Several moisturizers feature at least one of these transformative ingredients, so how do you know which one is right for you? To help narrow down your options, we asked Adisa, along with other dermatologists, for their thoughts on some of the best moisturizers for mature skin.

Neutrogena continuously proves that you don't have to spend a ton of money to reap the benefits of wrinkle-smoothing ingredients. This night cream targets the primary causes of wrinkles, uneven tone, and loss of elasticity with a firming and plumping lipid complex called Hexinol, brightening vitamin C, and moisturizing ingredients such as shea butter and glycerin.

Key ingredients: vitamin C, glycerin, hexinol

The clinical studies on this gel cream after four weeks of use are pretty impressive. Co-signed by dermatologist Hadley King, this moisturizer works at a cellular level to restimulate collagen production, diminish dark spots, and significantly smooth deep-set wrinkles.

“It contains humectants and emollients like squalane, as well as niacinamide, which improves tone and texture, and willowherb, which has senolytic properties to help promote skin longevity,” says King. The brand's proprietary combination of plant stem cells targets exosomes, energizes skin through NAD+, and decreases inflammation.

Key ingredients: squalane, niacinamide, willowherb, plant stem cells

Bring the spa home: This do-it-all hydrator makes it a breeze to give mature skin a refresher. Valmont’s Prime Renewing Pack leaves skin with a healthy glow in minutes and uses a creamy formula that reduces fine lines and wrinkles while seamlessly smoothing texture. Use a thin layer of the Pack as a quick five-minute mask, or a thicker layer for a more intensive treatment.

Key ingredients: peptides, RNA liposome, triple DNA complex

Sisley Paris's Black Rose cream is a go-to choice for beauty lovers and makeup artists alike, thanks to its excellent priming capabilities. The star ingredients, alkekengi calyx extract and padina pavonica, work in tandem to plump skin that's lost volume. There's also hibiscus flower extract and alpine rose extract, which help erase dullness, making your skin look more vibrant and glowy before you apply any makeup.

Key ingredients: black rose oil extract, black rose aqueous extract, alpine rose extract, alkekengi calyx extract, padina pavonica extract, hibiscus flower extract, shea and camelina oils

If you’re looking for a longer-lasting alternative for skin hydration, allow us to introduce you to Neuraé’s Harmonie The Sleeping Mask. The nourishing mask is created to rejuvenate dull, dry skin while reducing redness over time. Plus, its emollient-rich formula is the perfect addition to your beauty routine in colder months.

Key ingredients: gardenia jasminoides extract, shea butter, kokum butter, babassu oil

Here’s a top-rated moisturizer from Sulwhasoo that minimizes dullness, fine lines, wrinkles, and dryness simultaneously. The hypoallergenic cream has a whipped texture that works for a range of skin types and delivers up to 24 hours of hydration.

Key ingredients: Korean herb extract, Jisun firming complex, white mulberry, jujube, Chinese skullcap

Augustinus Bader is another worthy splurge, and in a few uses, you'll understand why. The Rich Cream utilizes a proprietary blend of TFC8, which features squalane, argan, sunflower seed, and evening primrose oils to stimulate cellular renewal and deliver targeted ingredients to skin cells.

Key ingredients: evening primrose oil, argan oil, sunflower seed oil, squalane, glycerin

If you’re already a fan of ZIIP’s devices, this moisturizer from the brand deserves a place in your rotation. The brand’s high-tech essential works with your skin’s electrical activity, along with ingredients like encapsulated collagen peptides, argireline, and more, to smooth expression lines while boosting firmness and radiance after six weeks of use.

Key ingredients: collagen peptide moisturizer with argireline amplified peptide solution, marine samphire bioactive

The 2:4:2 in the names refers to the trifecta of two percent ceramides, four percent cholesterol, and two percent fatty acids that work together to rebalance the skin and even out elasticity irregularities that cause sagging, roughness, and enlarged pores. It's a deeply moisturizing formula that replenishes and seals in moisture to help skin look and feel smooth and plump.

Key ingredients: ceramides, cholesterol, fatty acids

You'll see impressive skin-firming results with many of the moisturizers on this list, but this one is specifically designed for those who don't want to wait weeks to reap the benefits of their cream. Over time, the formula stimulates the sirtuin protein within the skin to boost collagen, hyaluronic acid, and elastin production.

Key ingredients: instant tightening sugars, micro-hyaluronic acid, glycerin, sunflower seed oil, grapeseed oil, shea butter

Dermatologist Marisa Garshick notes that some moisturizers contain ingredients that help support collagen production, thereby improving the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles while firming the skin. “A great option is the Mother Science Molecular Genesis barrier repair moisturizer, which uses their patented ingredient malassezin to strengthen the skin barrier, while also containing other ingredients like hyaluronic acid, growth factors, copper peptides, lipids and squalane, which together help to boost hydration, reduce the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles, and offer antioxidant benefits against free radical damage,” she explains.

Key ingredients: malassezin, copper peptides, squalane, hyaluronic acid, lipid concentrate

Mature skin can still experience breakouts, but this formula, which King also recommends, replenishes lost moisture without clogging pores or making the skin oilier. The formula looks to dimethicone for moisture and adaptogenic Chinese herbs to strengthen compromised skin barriers.

Key ingredients: dimethicone, ginseng, gotu kola, dongquai

Another one of Adisa's top choices is this rejuvenating cream from Andalou Naturals. The formula utilizes bakuchiol, a gentler yet equally effective alternative to retinol. “Bakuchiol works to reduce the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles and overall leaves the skin feeling rejuvenated,” Adisa explains. It also features a fruit stem cell complex and super antioxidants to restore and protect the skin barrier. "This has a nice, lightweight whipped consistency that quickly absorbs," notes Adisa.

Key ingredients: bakuchiol, resveratrol, goji berry, aloe vera

This rich moisturizer, created by dermatologist Ben Johnson, is rich in barrier-protecting oils and antioxidants. The cream also contains an effective blend of phosphatidylcholine, raspberry seed oil, and shea butter to keep skin nourished. However, if you require even more moisture, consider layering the brand's face oil, Osmosis Nourish, on top after application.

Key ingredients: phosphatidylcholine, raspberry seed oil, shea butter, apricot oil, blueberry

Here's a top-rated cream that works overnight to smooth and tighten as you snooze. The formula not only evens skin's texture but also repairs its barrier while visibly lifting sagging skin. Plus, since you won't need more than two pumps of the favorite to cover your entire face, a little goes a long way.

Key ingredients: retinol, bakuchioil, vitamin C, hyaluronic acid, antioxidants

Adisa noted earlier that retinoids or the plant-based alternative, bakuchiol, are ideal for improving and preventing fine lines, increasing cellular turnover, smoothing skin texture, and enhancing elasticity—so check ingredient lists for these. Garshick says to also look for moisturizers like hyaluronic acid and glycerin, along with ingredients to support the skin barrier, such as ceramides and squalane, and peptides or growth factors to help with firmness. “Antioxidants like vitamin C can help to protect against environmental stressors, while niacinamide may be added to help calm and soothe the skin,” Garshick notes.

“As we age, the skin barrier weakens, contributing to increased moisture loss, and there is decreased production of sebum, hyaluronic acid, and ceramides, key ingredients that help to keep the skin moisturized. This makes the skin more prone to dryness and dullness,” Garshick explains.

Johnson adds that skin dries out with age primarily due to a loss of circulation. “As the skin ages, it loses about one percent of circulation each adult year, so at 50, the skin has 25 percent less circulation,” says Johnson. “This leads to fewer nutrients, lipids, and immune cells, which result in a porous, dehydrated barrier.”

Incorporating a moisturizer for mature skin helps hydrate the skin while supporting the skin barrier. “Some moisturizers may also contain ingredients that help to support collagen production, improving the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles while also firming the skin,” notes Garshick.

Johnson advises steering clear of alpha-hydroxy acids because they can dry out and damage the skin. Ceramides, while generally good for sealing in moisture, can also be problematic. “They have a feedback-loop effect on the skin that results in them slowing down your epidermal turnover rate,” the expert says. You should also ease off on harsh exfoliants, which can damage the skin barrier and speed up moisture loss, leaving skin dry and crepey.

#6826. Was There a Fall on ‘Dancing With the Stars’ Disney Night, or Was It on Purpose? Dancer Diego Pasillas Speaks Out!

Dancing With the Stars delivered a truly epic series of performances during Disney Night, including a packed opening number complete with a Newsies segment. And the whole thing was so well-choreographed that one of the dancers fell over and recovered so flawlessly that everyone thought it was on purpose.

While there was some debate from fans online, turns out this fall was, in fact, a fall. Dancer Diego Pasillas confirmed as much on Instagram, saying “You guys. I just ate it on national television.” He then did an example of the correct move, recreated his fall, and said “the show must go on.”

Diego’s comments were filled with praise and support from his fellow Dancing With the Stars cast members, including Hailey Bills, who wrote “BUT THE WAY YOU GOT UP,” and Val Chmerkovskiy who commented, “Bro you shablammed it and then shamblammed it in the air.”

Reminder: pro Adele Zaikman had to unexpectedly skip Disney Night due to being sick, and The Sun reports that her absence “left producers and dancers racing to rework multiple performances,” with the rehearsals “becoming even more chaotic” since she was booked for three different dances.

“She had to drop out very last minute, so that mixed with the week already being one day shorter has been really hectic behind the scenes,” a source said. “Multiple troupe members are going to fill in for her. The opening number was the big one. The other two she just had a tiny part, so it wasn’t as big of a deal to fill. But the troupe has been practicing all Monday before showtime.”

In other words, this was a slightly last-minute routine without a lot of practice time!

#6825. All the Highlights From Lagos Fashion Week 2026

The 16th edition of Lagos Fashion Week celebrated sustainable circularity and local talent.

The spring/summer 2027 fashion month may have come to a close in the major cities, but it’s just getting started everywhere else. Over the course of this past weekend, designers and industry insiders came together in Lagos, Nigeria, for the 16th edition of Lagos Fashion Week. Workshops, panels, and presentations focused on sustainability and local talent, alongside a strong lineup of emerging designers and Nigerian household names. Above all else, the designers demonstrated a commitment to craft and heritage. As Aisha Ayensu, the designer behind Christie Brown, said during a panel discussion on day one: “It is necessary to build a sustainable relationship with African artisans, because the success of any African fashion brand relies on the craft people.”

Global favorite Orange Culture (which has shown internationally at New York and London Fashion Weeks) kicked off the season in the city. Curious about the other moments that happened during Lagos Fashion Week? Read on for some of the designers to know from the new season.

Founded in 2015 by Segun Aladejana, Sevon Dejana puts an emphasis on high-drama evening wear with a sculptural, artistic nod. Think: gowns covered in explosive florals and feathers, but sculpted in an unusual way, with a modernist take on traditional haute couture shapes. The label is for the bold, and there is certainly no lack of embellishment or color, as was the case for the new collection presented on the runway for the new season.

Hertunba puts an emphasis on African craft with experimental, sculptural silhouettes. Founder Florentina Agu started taking dressmaking classes at the age of 13, and officially started her line in 2020. The brand makes a point of employing (and training) local women to sew and weave and also reduces waste. 90 percent of Hertunba’s excess materials are repurposed into handcrafted interior décor like rugs and throw pillows, or are pieced together into new, wearable pieces.

Designed between Lagos and London, PiiLLz and PoiZn takes a slow fashion approach to maximalism. Designer Ifeoma Umenyiora creates pieces that embrace stripes, sequins, and feathers, always with a focal point on color. The label maintains its local craft by offering bespoke tailored gowns and other pieces, all made on-site in its atelier in Lagos.

Launched as a sustainable, gender-neutral line by Adedamola Adebayo, most of Bornstar Nigeria’s pieces are made of fabric that is manufactured locally in Nigeria. The brand’s relaxed tailoring is a colorful new take on Nigerian style. Details like intricate beadwork, crochet, and tassels accent the streamlined, tailored separates, and accessories, like chunky bags, are also strong.

Founded in 2021 by designer Adedayo Ilori, Dimeji Ilori upcycles old pieces to create new garments that are collaged, cut, bleached, and transformed into new tactile wonders. Texture is definitely at the center of the aesthetic. Think: a fluffy hat and matching trims or dresses fully embellished with cowrie shells, as shown above.

#6823. 13 Things You Should Toss ASAP If You’ve Lived in Your Home for More Than 5 Years

You'll feel a wave of relief once you do it.

It may seem like you just moved into your new home or apartment yesterday. But in a flash, five years have passed and you’re out of storage and feeling overwhelmed. Most of us know it doesn’t take long for our belongings to pile up.

“By year five, the ‘endless storage’ honeymoon is over,” says Ann Lightfoot, cofounder, DDH Home Organizing & Move Management. “Your stuff has multiplied, your square footage hasn’t, and you now know how you actually live in your space.”

But now is the ideal time for a thoughtful edit. “Taking stock after five years gives us a chance to see what's actually taking up space, what we're still using, and what we've simply gotten used to storing,” says Laura Kinsella, founder of Urban OrgaNYze. “Our careers, families, routines, and interests can change dramatically, but the things we've accumulated don't automatically change with us.”

Decluttering also enhances good design. “To me, a beautifully designed home isn't about how much you can fit into it. It's about being intentional with what you choose to keep,” says Kerrie Kelly, NKBA, FASID, CEO and creative director of Kerrie Kelly Studio, and NKBA 2026 National Board Chair. “Editing isn't about deprivation; it's about making room for what you actually love and use.”

If you don’t know where to begin, start small. “Edit by category rather than trying to tackle the entire house in a weekend,” says Kelly. “Start with one drawer, one cabinet, or one closet. Take everything out, clean the space, and put back only what deserves to be there. It's amazing how quickly those small decisions transform the way a home feels. Empty space is a luxury, too!”

Ahead, here’s what design and organizational experts say you should get rid of if you’ve been in your home five years or more.

“Five years of conferences, events, fundraisers, and corporate gifting can leave you with an impressive collection of tote bags, reusable water bottles, and branded merchandise you never actually set out to own,” says Kinsella. Keep what you genuinely like and use, and pass along the extras.

Most manuals live online now, and that tangle of chargers likely powers nothing in your house. Recycle them responsibly, says Lightfoot.

“Threadbare towels, mismatched sheets, and extra bedding you never reach for take up valuable storage space,” says Kelly. “Keep a thoughtfully curated collection of beautiful, functional linens and recycle the worn-out ones where possible.”

“If your spin shoes haven't been touched since lockdown and the exercise bike has become ‘the chair’ in your bedroom, you may be dedicating valuable space to a workout routine that's no longer part of your life,” says Kinsella. Sell or donate equipment you no longer use so you have room for the activities you truly enjoy now.

Craft kits, furniture you planned to refinish, the bread maker and so on: “If someday hasn’t arrived in five years, give yourself permission to let it go, guilt-free,” says Lightfoot.

“Those little objects that have been sitting on a shelf for years can become visual clutter,” says Kelly. “I love a meaningful collection, but every piece should earn its place. Sometimes removing three things makes the one beautiful object remaining look even better.”

“That rug or wall art you swore would work ‘somewhere’ has spent five years leaning against a wall. It’s time to release it to a home where it fits,” says Lightfoot.

Hang on to a small labeled paint sample for touch-ups—and maybe one tile. But the rest, including the dried-up products or the five half-empty cans in the garage or basement, can go to your town’s hazardous waste drop-off, says Lightfoot.

This is a no-judgment zone: “If the avocado slicer, specialty pan, or oversized appliance hasn't made an appearance in years, it probably doesn't deserve prime real estate in your kitchen,” says Kelly. Ditto for duplicate kitchen tools.

“Whether it's a juicer, red light mask, or massage tool, we've all had that new wellness routine we're completely committed to for a few weeks before life gets busy and the habit quietly falls away,” says Kinsella. “The problem is that the gadget doesn't disappear when the enthusiasm does, and five years later, you may have an entire cabinet devoted to routines that lasted a month.” It’s fine to try something new to learn it wasn't for you, but you don't have to keep storing the equipment forever.

Maybe it's an extra accent chair that collects clothes or a side table that's always in the way. Just because something fits physically doesn't mean it belongs. Give your rooms space to breathe and your furniture room to shine, says Kelly.

Purge the old sunscreen, crusty nail polish, expired makeup, shampoo you don’t like, and half-used cleaners that have piled up in your bathroom. Toss them safely and enjoy the instant breathing room, says Lightfoot.

“If you've lived in your home for five years and still have boxes you never unpacked, it's a pretty good indication that you haven't needed most of what's inside on a regular basis,” says Kinsella. “Open the box, decide what's worth keeping, and find those belongings a proper home so you're not continuing to live around a move that’s already happened.”

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.
