#6799. What Kind of Power Player Are You, According to Your Zodiac Sign?

The cutthroat lawyers of “All’s Fair” provide more than just fashion inspiration and captivating plot twists.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

The cutthroat lawyers of All's Fair provide more than just fashion inspiration and captivating plot twists. Each character also embodies one or more zodiac signs. Together, the power players of Grant, Ronson, & Greene represent the 12 signs’ best traits—and some of their not-so-nice ones, too. As the legal drama returns November 4 on Hulu for another season of scandals, secrets, and stilettos, we’ll get to see cunning worthy of a Scorpio, ambition that would impress a Capricorn, and diplomacy that any Libra would applaud. Find your legal avatar below, and as Carr would say, “See you in court.”

Aries is assertive, ambitious, and impulsive. They go after what they want, even if it means being the bad guy. And if nobody wants to join them, they’ll do their own thing. The first sign of the zodiac, Aries is ruled by Mars, the planet of war—a fitting influence for their ability to turn any courtroom into a battlefield.

A born romantic who yearns for a life of luxury—that’s the earth sign Taurus. But it’s not all red roses and dreamy silk gowns. Taurus also shows determination and a serious work ethic; the sign is known for its stubborn pursuit of goal.

Gemini is the hyper-intelligent sign of communication. They not only find out information, but strategically choose how and when to reveal it. Gemini loves to make a statement, whether with what they say or what they wear.

Cancer feels everything fully, and their big heart can often lead them to form bonds with unexpected people. At the same time, their mood can also cause drama. Like Emerald, this water sign’s empathetic nature makes it easy to relate to others and understand where they’re coming from—even if their lives and personalities are totally different.

Creative and charismatic, Leo is the natural leader of any group. In fact, they command every room they’re in. Leo’s confidence means they find it easy to finalize a deal and they don’t second-guess themselves. Leo also loves a bold look and has a bit of a vain side.

Though quieter than others at the firm, Liberty is just as intelligent. Her strategic mind is what makes her an excellent Virgo. Liberty finds loopholes in the law and notices details others miss, making winning cases a breeze. Virgo is one of the hardest-working signs of the zodiac, and Liberty is one of the hardest-working lawyers in LA. Virgo is also one of the most caring signs, as exemplified by Liberty’s commitment to empowering women.

Represented by the scales of justice, Libra is the sign of equality and fairness. They’re literally the sign of the legal field. As a veteran lawyer, Dina knows the legal field like the back of her hand. Her passion for law drives her work, and her diplomacy helps her connect with both her coworkers and their clients.

Scorpio is all about power—who has it, who wants it, and how to use it. We can see this in both Allura and Carr, two ambitious Scorpios who often find themselves on opposite sides of a power struggle. Scorpios treat clothing like armor, and both women do just that by cultivating a glamorous, intimidating wardrobe.

Sagittarius thinks outside the box. Like Milan, they’re willing to go off script and try wild ideas that might seem out-there to others. To them, the end result justifies the means, and if an eccentric tactic accomplishes their goals, that’s enough of a reason to try it out.

The most ambitious sign of the zodiac, Capricorn will stop at nothing to get what they want. Hard-working and intelligent, they’re more motivated by material gains than intangible ideals. And their intensity can be a little scary. But as any Capricorn knows, whatever it takes to succeed, it’s worth it.

As the sign of innovation, Aquarius loves nothing more than breaking with tradition and setting out independently. When the old ways don’t work, they create a new way. Aquarius is also quite independent; they’re often more comfortable alone rather than relying on others.

Pisces is the last sign of the zodiac, a placement that gives it wisdom. They often find themselves in a mentor role, inspiring the younger people who follow in their career footsteps. Pisces is always ready to share advice or listen without judgment. They have a strong sense of creativity and a willingness to think outside the box.

Season 2 of the legal drama premieres November 4 on Hulu

#6798. The Midi Bob Is the Sleek, High-Shine Haircut That Flatters 50-Plus Hair

This elegant style will be everywhere this fall.

Finding a hairstyle more sought-after than a well-cut midi bob (a long bob that brushes the shoulders or collarbones but delivers a blunt, full effect) is no small feat. Favored by celebrities such as Pamela Anderson, Halle Berry, Hailey Bieber, Kaia Gerber, and Julianne Moore, this shoulder-grazing style has become something of a signature among A-listers in recent years. Coincidentally, it is also one of the most flattering cuts for anyone over 50.

Most recently, Claudia Winkleman has brought the midi bob back into the spotlight, as her bold signature hairstyle continues to make headlines as the Celebrity Traitors hits screens.

As the name suggests, the lob offers a middle ground between long hair and a bob. It falls anywhere between the chin and the collarbone and is often seen as a transitional style for those looking to go shorter gradually.

“Hitting just at the collarbone, the lob offers the best of both worlds: the ease of a bob with the styling flexibility of longer hair,” says Elliot Bute, hairstylist at Hershesons Fitzrovia. “It’s incredibly versatile, particularly for anyone who wants the ease of a shorter haircut but still wants to be able to tie their hair back.”

He adds that the cut works across most face shapes because you can adjust where the length sits and where you introduce layers or movement. Take Julianne Moore, for example. Her blunt cut creates glossy fullness in her natural hair, while fellow actor Winona Ryder has adopted the style in a more undone, choppy way.

Remember, depending on your lob style, your styling routine will look different, as Tim Scott-Wright, owner of Tim Scott-Wright at The Hair Surgery, explains here.

“The lob is a strong, on-trend look that is great with a wave,” he says. “It’s kind of an in-between look and is best worn wavy with texture. It’s a way of modernizing lacklustre long hair without losing all your length. Cate Blanchett and Reese Witherspoon have both recently sported this look.”

Consider investing in a texture spray or air-dry cream if your hair has natural shape and movement, or use a curling tong or straightener to create soft, natural bevels.

#6794. All About Charles Leclerc and Alexandra Leclerc’s Relationship Timeline Now That They’re New Parents

It’s not a secret that Formula 1 is full of some of the world’s most eligible bachelors—but it’s also a hub for some super adorable couples, like F1 driver Charles Leclerc and his wife Alexandra Saint Mleux, aka mom and dad to baby Bianca!

Didn't realize your F1 crush was spoken for? Well, that might be because since Charles and Alexandra, an influencer and Rhode ambassador, were first spotted together in late 2023, they've mostly avoided attending public events as a couple and only walked a few red carpets together. They also rarely post about each other on social media, choosing instead to keep their romance private—but now that they’re parents, we might start to get more intel!

Need to know more? Here's a breakdown of Charles Leclerc and Alexandra Saint Mleux's relationship timeline.

Fans started suspecting that something was going on between Charles and Alexandra in early 2023, after the two were spotted together at Paris Fashion Week. At the time, Alexandra's social media was reportedly private and Charles didn't address the speculation until a few months later.

Charles eventually put all the rumors to rest, confirming that he was dating someone in an interview in May 2023. Though he didn’t name Alexandra at the time, the two made their debut as a couple a few months later.

The duo indulged in some light PDA while attending Day 8 of the 2023 Wimbledon Tennis Championships. The two sat next to fellow F1 driver Pierre Gasly and his girlfriend Francisca Gomes.

Alexandra and Charles looked all loved up at the Lakers vs. Portland Trail Blazers in Los Angeles.

Then they continued their relationship tour, appearing together at the Turkish Airlines EuroLeague basketball game between AS Monaco and Olympiacos Piraeus. They were snapped sitting on the sidelines enjoying the game. Again, not a hugely romantic outing, but also one very deliberately captured on camera.

Cute alert! In April 2024, Charles announced that he had adopted a puppy with an adorable Instagram post. The carousel of pics mostly featured “baby Leo” living his best puppy life, but one shot in particular shows him attempting to chew what looks like a woman's hand. Could it be Alexandra? (All signs point to: yes.)

Meanwhile, Alexandra posted a TikTok video of her snuggling up to her new fur-baby. And she and their pup have been F1 regulars ever since.

After watching Charles win the Monaco Grand Prix for the very first time, Alexandra joined her partner at the official Gala Dinner—a rare red carpet outing for the two. She also appears briefly in Charles' official YouTube vlog of the race.

The duo arrived hand in hand at a screening of F1 The Movie in Monaco, showing the public that they're still going strong. Sadly, Leo was not invited.

The two took to Instagram to share the happy news with a series of photos captioned “Mr and Mrs Leclerc”…and yes, Leo was included in those pics, too.

Surprise! Charles and Alexandra tied the knot in Monaco with a civil ceremony at Villa La Vigie (and of course, Leo was part of the celebration).

The couple sparked pregnancy rumors when they showed up at the Hungarian Grand Prix with Leo in tow...as well as a possible baby bump.

At last, Alexandra (seemingly) confirmed pregnancy rumors (while posing on a yacht, naturally).

Alexandra and Charles announce their daughter’s arrival with a simple joint Instagram post, captioned, “Bianca 🤍 3.11.26.”

#6793. Earthy Neutral Jeans Make Cold-Weather Outfits Feel Instantly Warmer

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

A richer alternative to classic blue denim, from cocoa wide-legs to sage straight-legs.

Listen, we love classic blue denim, but as fashion obsessives, we also believe in changing things up. If you want to drift away from black and indigo this season, consider jeans in earthy neutrals to bring a fresh outlook to your fall and winter wardrobe. The palette runs wide: chocolaty browns, soft sands, light stone blues, olive, rust, sage, and even dusty pink.

In addition to their warmth, what we love about these shades is how easily they work. Think of them as neutrals with more personality: a pair of cocoa wide-legs looks sharp with a cream turtleneck and loafers, while sage straight-legs soften an oversize charcoal blazer. Look for a rich, slightly variable dye and a sturdy cotton twill that will hold its shape and fade gracefully, then style them as you would your favorite blues, with a little more intention and a lot more warmth.

Below, we’ve rounded up some of the best earth-toned jeans to pair with your favorite fall/winter pieces.

These jeans in a cool-toned brown called “Washed Canteen Clean” pair a highly versatile earth-toned shade with a flared hem. Wear them with your favorite purple-toned knit and black ankle boots for a look that feels moody, polished, and effortlessly autumnal.

This relaxed straight-leg silhouette has a vintage-inspired shape that feels thoroughly modern, done in a light, marbled khaki wash.

Along with a wide-leg silhouette, this pair in the shade Great Basin, a hue reminiscent of muddy waters, brings a rich, moody depth to your denim rotation. Style them with a cobalt cable-knit and tan suede boots for a tonal look that feels on-trend and a little rugged.

Drawstring toggles at both hems add a subtle, unexpected sporty detail (hence the name) to a relaxed jean. Team them with your favorite court sneakers for an elevated athletic-leaning look.

In the shade “Tobacco,” these jeans are built to last a lifetime. The wide-leg fit and timeless color will earn a spot in your rotation all year long.

Barrel-leg jeans continue to be popular this season, yet this soft sepia shade trades the trend’s usual denim blue for a softer, warmer, more grounded take. Pair them with a fitted black bodysuit and pointed-toe boots to balance the roomy shape with something sleek.

Something about this shape and earthy shade brings a sense of calm and ease to our hectic everyday lives. It’s the perfect jean for slipping into a relaxed, weekend mindset.

Made of soft-stretch denim, this brown jean features a high-waisted, straight-leg shape that flatters without feeling restrictive. Style it with a tucked-in white tee, a shearling coat, and loafers for a polished, easy outfit that works from the office to the weekend.

A barrel leg in a vintage-inspired brown wash gives these jeans a lived-in, worn-in feel from the very first wear. Style them with a slim ribbed tank, a cropped leather jacket, and sling-back flats to keep the roomy shape looking sharp.

When you think of an everyday jean, perhaps the color army green doesn’t immediately come to mind. But once you see how versatile this shade can be, it’s determined to win a permanent spot in your rotation.

This slim palazzo jean brings ’70s vibes and softness to a fall color palette. Wear it with a rust-colored turtleneck, a tan suede jacket, and platform boots for a retro-leaning outfit that feels warm, confident, and distinctly seasonal.

#6787. This Brand-New Illinois Farmhouse Was Designed to Look Hundreds of Years Old

The team at Buccellato Design and Architecture focused on incorporating heritage details and timeworn furnishings.

For Aimee and Kevin Buccellato, the best compliment they could receive about a project isn’t, “Wow, it looks so different in here.” It’s that a home looks like they haven’t changed a thing.

On a country road in Momence, Illinois, a beloved farmhouse stands as a timeworn landmark for the surrounding agricultural community. For the homeowners—a retired couple returning to their Midwestern roots—the property was as much a repository of memories as it was a place to create new ones. “Having grown up nearby, the property evoked a strong sense of memory and place,” says Aimee, who founded Buccellato Design and Architecture alongside her husband, Kevin. “At the outset of the project, and throughout, our clients wanted the new house to feel like it had always been there, so much so that perhaps the neighbors could be led to believe that the original house remained intact, yet amended by skillful renovation and discreet additions.”

It was an architectural sleight of hand rooted in a distinctly Midwestern philosophy: “Be good to the neighbors.” Alongside the Buccellatos, the couple approached the 6,000-square-foot rebuild with the restraint of preservationists and the imagination of storytellers. “Great care was taken to instill the home and outbuildings with a sense of history and rootedness, creating a home that feels as though it’s been part of this bucolic setting for centuries,” says Kevin of the project, which also included the restoration of the property’s original 19th-century barn and corn crib. “We drew inspiration from traditional farm complexes, where homesteads were added slowly over time, reconnecting the site with its Folk-Victorian-Farm-House and agricultural building roots.”

Inside, the narrative continues through antique white oak floors, salvaged beams, heritage florals, and rooms that borrow their palettes from the landscape. In the kitchen, a wall of windows replaces upper cabinetry, framing sunrise over the barn and walnut trees. The glass porch offers sweeping views of the Kankakee River, while a tucked-away speakeasy recalls the original farmhouse’s stone foundation.

For Kevin and Aimee, the guiding principle was a new construction home that felt “sensitive, not wasteful, polite, not ostentatious,” an exercise in knowing when architecture and interiors should defer to their surroundings. The result is a house with an invented past and a very real sense of belonging that does the Midwest—and the neighbors—proud.

FAST FACTSDesigner: Buccellato Design and ArchitectureStylist: Cate RaganLocation: Momence, IllinoisThe Space: A four-bedroom, four-and-a-half-bathroom home across 6,000 square feet

The entryway offers a fitting introduction to the home'’s carefully constructed sense of history, replete with nickel gap paneling, fumed and waxed antique white oak floors, and a light-filled staircase. Even the furnishings—including a vintage Persian rug and antique Jacobean gateleg table—suggest a collection acquired over generations.

Benjamin Moore’s Nelson Blue sets a serene backdrop for the sitting room, where traditional silhouettes and understated patterns make an easy pairing. “This room was designed to honor the history of this property and embody the love that our clients hold for this special place,” adds Aimee.

A tufted mohair sofa, realized in a rich umber, lends a sense of history, while an iconic feather lamp by Bunny Williams offers a reminder that even the most classic rooms benefit from a little irreverence.

In the kitchen, the Buccellatos traded upper cabinets for an expansive wall of windows overlooking the farmland, relocating the practical necessities to an adjacent dish room and larder. Salvaged white oak beams and barnboards overhead reinforce the home’s agricultural roots, while furniture-like cabinetry and leathered black quartzite countertops strike a timeless note.

With natural light pouring in from three sides, the dining room is what the Buccellatos describe as “peaceful, soft, and ethereal,” equally suited to both morning coffee and candlelit dinners. A dreamy mural wallpaper by Susan Harter references the property’s location along the Kankakee River, while formal seating lends a sense of occasion without sacrificing the home’s easygoing country charm.

Nestled among mature trees, the primary bedroom feels almost suspended in the surrounding landscape. The Buccellatos leaned into that sense of serenity with the palette. “It’s a dreamlike mix of earthy neutrals and pale blues that paints a serene backdrop for elegant details, vintage furnishings, and a delicate mix of subtle pattern,” adds Kevin.

A French country canopy bed, dressed in tailored Schumacher linen, takes center stage, while vintage-inspired furnishings and subtle pattern keep the room from feeling overly precious. An adjacent sitting room offers a place to read the morning paper or unwind with a cup of tea in the evenings.

In the vestibule, a little pattern goes a long way. A climbing floral from Schumacher in inky charcoal makes a charming counterpoint to the bedroom’s hushed palette, while a checked window bench is the ideal spot to read, giving the passageway purpose.

Pale blue walls and creamy millwork lend the primary bathroom an old-fashioned prettiness. A custom vanity introduces a tailored architectural element, while a Cowtan & Tout floral Roman shade softens the room’s more utilitarian details.

Designed as a playful respite for guests and future grandchildren, this bedroom plays off pattern and primary hues. “The red painted woodwork feels unexpected and cheerful against the blue-and-white wallpaper, striped rug, and patterned bedding,” says Aimee.

“The brick-clad main block echoes the footprint of the original farmhouse on this site, yet the story we wrote for the house begins with the folk Victorian wing, inspired by L-shaped farmhouses of that period,” says Kevin. “In our modern narrative, the clapboard-sided wing came first, followed by the brick-clad ’addition,’ which takes inspiration from the later Colonial Revival period in its scale and sophistication of detailing.”

Founded by Aimee and Kevin Buccellato, Buccellato Design & Architecture is a practice marked by twenty-plus years of close collaboration, in architectural practice and in life. With projects on both coasts and throughout the country, the firm’s award-winning work reveals a dedication to craft, artistry, and detail. Achieved in partnership with clients, their projects reflect a purposeful sense of rootedness in the landscape and abiding devotion to beauty and the comfort of home.

#6786. Van Cleef & Arpels Announces Support for Montecito’s Lotusland

The jewelry house heads to Santa Barbara with a fitting gift from a brand captivated by botanic motifs.

Above: The Rose Garden Terrace at Lotusland in Montecito, CA.

Ganna Walska was a lifelong collector. First, there were the husbands: six of them, from a Russian baron to a yoga instructor. Then came the jewelry: hundreds of carats of emeralds, diamonds, and sapphires. Finally, there were plants, her love of which ultimately led her to create a 37-acre botanical garden in Montecito, California.

Walska, an opera singer born Hanna Puacz in Poland in 1887, purchased the Cuesta Linda estate in 1941. She intended to both live there and house Tibetan monks, but after her final divorce (and after the monks failed to show), Walska devoted herself to collecting rare plants. She renamed the property Lotusland, for the Indian lotus in one of the ponds, and poured her fortune into the project. In 1971 she auctioned off many of her jewels to fund her gardens. One piece was the Oiseau clip from Van Cleef & Arpels, a special order depicting a bird in flight carrying a 96.62-carat yellow diamond known as the “Walska Briolette.”

Following her death, the property was opened to the public, and now Van Cleef is making a return. The maison has announced its support of Lotusland, focusing in particular on the Rose Garden Terrace. It’s a gift that honors both Walska’s love of the brand and the inspiration that Van Cleef has pulled from nature for a century, from countless floral variations to the butterfly motif. Jewels will once again endow Lotusland—just as Ganna would have wanted.

#6785. Dia Art Foundation and Chanel Celebrate Steve McQueen and Roni Horn

Sofia Coppola, Julio Torres, and Marion Cotillard also attended the gala.

As co-chairs of Dia Fall Night 2026, Carrie Mae Weems, Rosemarie Trockel, and Matthieu Blazy hosted an amazing roster of guests to celebrate the Dia Art Foundation’s initiatives. Sofia Coppola, Marion Cotillard, Julio Torres, and artists Chase Hall, Marina Abramović, Garrett Bradley, and Cecily Brown were among those who turned out for the Chanel-sponsored event, which honored artists Steve McQueen and Roni Horn. Both McQueen and Horn have created installations shown at Dia Beacon, Dia’s most well-known exhibition space. It also served as a goodbye to Dia director Jessica Morgan, who will leave the organization after over a decade to serve as director of the Tate.

The evening began at Dia’s Chelsea galleries, where the show “David Lamelas: The Machine” was on display. Guests then made their way north a few blocks for cocktails and dinner. During the meal, artist Matthew Barney introduced Horn, his longtime friend, and reflected on a memory of explaining her work to his young daughter. In tribute to McQueen, musician and poet Meshell Ndegeocello made her way around the room performing a hymn and a striking spoken-word piece. (She collaborated with McQueen on the sound component of his 2024 Dia Beacon work “Bass.”) She was accompanied by singers Jade Hicks and Marsha Deboe and guitarist Chris Bruce.

Chanel and Blazy’s partnership with Dia is part of the brand’s continued support of arts organizations, which includes working with museums like the National Portrait Gallery in London and the Centre Pompidou in Paris.

#6782. When Is ‘Rivals’ Season 2, Part 2 Out? Full Release Schedule and How to Watch

Rutshire’s messiest residents are back for more drama.

The shoulder pads are back, the champagne is flowing, and the drama in Rutshire is showing absolutely no signs of slowing down.

If you've been counting down the days until Rivals returns, you're certainly not alone. Disney+'s gloriously scandalous adaptation of Jilly Cooper's best-selling novel has given us everything we could possibly want from a period drama: outrageous affairs, ruthless power plays, spectacular ‘80s fashion, and a cast of characters who are almost incapable of behaving themselves.

But rather than dropping every episode at once, Disney+ has split season 2 into two parts, leaving viewers with a wait between installments.

So, when exactly does Rivals season 2, part 2 arrive, and how many episodes are left? Here's everything you need to know.

The wait is almost over! Rivals season 2 part 2 will premiere on Disney+ on Wednesday, November 11, 2026, with three brand-new episodes landing at once.

That's right, we'll be getting a triple helping of Rutshire scandal before the series switches to weekly releases for its final three episodes.

The second season consists of 12 episodes in total, with the first six having aired earlier this year.

And following THAT devastating mid-season cliffhanger, there's plenty of unfinished business to get through.

Disney+ will release the remaining six episodes across four Wednesdays, with the season finale arriving in December.

So, you'll have three episodes to binge on November 11, followed by one new episode every Wednesday until the finale.

*Warning: Spoilers for season 2, part 1 ahead!

When we last visited Rutshire, things had taken a devastating turn.

Lady Monica Baddingham (Claire Rushbrook) was killed in a car accident after discovering that her husband Tony (David Tennant) had been having an affair with Maud O'Hara (Victoria Smurfit).

And with Tony now grieving, the battle between Corinium Television and rival company Venturer looks set to become even more ruthless.

Meanwhile, Rupert Campbell-Black (Alex Hassell) is facing some difficult decisions of his own, particularly when it comes to his relationship with Taggie O'Hara (Bella Maclean).

According to Disney+, the second half of the season will see affairs unravel, alliances fracture, and long-buried secrets threaten to come to light.

Expect plenty more ’80s excess, from lavish parties and the Hampshire Hunt Ball to Bonfire Night celebrations, and a particularly chaotic Christmas.

There are also some new faces joining the drama, including Rachael Stirling as Monica's younger sister Araminta Pemberton, Rupert Evans as headmaster David Hawkley, and Santiago Cabrera as Argentinian polo player Alejandro Mendoza.

In other words, Rutshire isn't about to get any quieter.

There's good news for anyone already dreading the season 2 finale.

Disney+ has officially renewed Rivals for a third season, with another 12 episodes on the way.

While there's no confirmed release date just yet, at least we know the scandals, romance, and spectacularly questionable decisions aren't ending with season 2.

Both seasons of Rivals are available on Disney+ and Hulu.

All eight episodes of season 1 and the first six episodes of season 1 are available to stream now, meaning there's still time to catch up before part 2 arrives.

And if you've somehow managed to avoid the drama until now, consider this your invitation to enter the wonderfully chaotic world of Rutshire.

#6781. 6 Antique Trends Designers Are Watching for 2027

Above: The kitchen in designer Phillip Thomas’s Bellport retreat.

Do the words “antique” and “trend” really belong in the same sentence? In many ways, a great antique shop feels like the antidote to trend fatigue: a space where the true must-haves are hidden in dusty corners, with part of the fun in the thrill of the hunt. Yet, as we round out 2026, there’s no denying the current design cycle is casting off perfection for patina. Antiques are en vogue, and top designers are keeping a close eye on the periods, pieces, and yes, trends, that are poised to find new life in 2027.

“The return of interest in antiques reflects a trend towards more personal, unique interiors, and away from strict modern rooms which can often be cool and generic,” says ELLE Decor A-List designer Jamie Drake. This shift paves the way for more playful interactions between pieces, unexpected layering, and unabashed personality.

Other experts second the motion for more character. “I’m seeing clients really drawn to antiques with more to them than meets the eye: pieces that have a bit of humor or whimsy, or pieces with great provenance,” says ELLE Decor Rising Star William Cullum. (He lists a “ridiculously giant butterfly light fixture” from New Orleans, rattan chairs from Bing Crosby’s estate, and a disco ball from Cyndi Lauper’s Bonham’s sale in his list of recent acquisitions—professional and personal.)

Luckily for the amateur antiquer, not all trending vintage styles require a substantial space commitment or glamorous provenance—although an interesting backstory helps. “Antiques are always about a story: where you found them, who owned them, and how that story will continue on after us,” Cullum says.

Ahead, we asked top designers and experts to share the most inspiring antique trends of the moment. From neoclassic mirrors to hand-painted chests paired with modern art, their takes are sure to inform your next estate sale rummage, or inspire you to restyle an old heirloom.

We called mirrored surfaces a major trend to watch for fall 2026, and antique trends have followed suit. Unexpected (or better yet, artfully manipulated) reflections can add rich dimension and movement to any space, but in this category, antiques create a window to the past that’s tough to beat.

“One of the easiest ways to add a sense of history to a room is to include an antique mirror,” says Drake. “Whether you choose a sober and strict neo-classic piece or a more flamboyant baroque one, reflections of the past add depth and sparkle.”

“One trend I’m observing is an uptick in antique dowry chests,” antiques dealer Ali Mahon says. From Pennsylvania Dutch to Swedish Folk and Spanish carved, she’s noticed clients gravitating toward more delicately adorned storage over spare. With thoughtfully mixed furniture (from style to period) becoming more desirable than matching sets, Mahon sees more hand-painted antique surfaces in our future: “With their decorative tulips, leaves, birds, and hearts, dowry chests create a colorful splash in any room.”

ELLE Decor Rising Star Reagan Gail Struble agrees—although delicate designs are not a requirement of the trend, in her book: “Something about a playful wash of color on an awesome old piece of furniture is very alluring, especially when you can see the age and texture of the wood peeking through,” she says. “Hand-painted case pieces add a little punch of charm to any room.”

Luckily, these antiques can be just as functional as they are fashionable. Mahon recommends using a painted chest as a coffee table, at the foot of the bed, or in a space that could benefit from a bit more romance (and a few extra drawers). When styling your find in a modern space, Struble says that contrast is key: try a “chippy Swedish chest of drawers” with an unexpected photograph or painting above it.

According to ELLE Decor A-List designer Nick Olsen, Art Deco pieces are setting the tone for 2027 antique trends—although, if you ask him, they were never out. “I'm particularly inspired by the [art deco influence in the] Gujarat palace in India,” he says. “I'm fascinated by how every element seems to have a wide border: marble floors, carpets, walls of veneer and mohair velvet upholstery.”

According to Olsen, using Art Deco design compositions can do as much for a room as actual pieces from the period. “I like the idea of contrasting borders: solid textures punctuated by smaller graphic prints on furniture and more delicate lighting,” he explains. And while borders are easy to create with paint, paneling, and carpet seaming, Olsen challenges clients to push the Art Deco agenda further by bordering with luxe materials.

Whether you’re new to antiquing or simply trying to add more character to a space, lighting is an excellent place to start. “Lighting is the jewelry of the home, so it’s one of my favorite places to bring in something vintage,” says A-Lister Corey Damen Jenkins. “An Art Deco sconce or an antique brass lantern has a warmth to it that’s hard to find in something brand new.”

Adding to an existing space? Jenkins advises starting small: “Swap one overhead fixture or put a pair of vintage lamps on a console, and you’ll be surprised how much the whole room shifts,” he says. “It’s an easy way to add history without starting over.”

“In light of America’s 250th birthday, Americana in general is in vogue,” says Mahon. Among her clients, the trend has manifested in quilts and textiles, portraits of sea captains, trade signs, and ironstone—culminating in an aesthetic she describes as “elevated country—not corny.”

If you’ve noticed that no particular design period seems to reign supreme among designers’ top antique trend picks, it’s not an accident. Heading into 2027, there’s a strong argument to be made that mixed-period design is more influential than any singular reference.

“I love a room that doesn’t belong to any one decade,” Jenkins says. “A nineteenth-century chest with a 1970s lamp on top, or a Lucite chair pulled up to an antique desk. Those are the pairings that make people stop and look twice.” Drake adds that seemingly out-of-place seating is an easy way to add intrigue. “I love including antique chairs as sculptural notes, contrasted against modern upholstery and tables,” he says.

But when it comes to antiquing, there’s really only so much one can plan for. Letting a great find lead the way often turns out to be a better strategy than arriving to an estate sale with a rigid checklist. The best way to keep up with top designers when it comes to antiquing this year? Find a piece that has something to say, place it where it seems to belong, and then try it where it doesn’t.

Grace McCarty is a freelance Associate Digital Editor at ELLE Decor, where she covers design trends, shopping, and culture. She previously held a staff position at SELF Magazine, where she focused on beauty, style, and wellness. Her work has also appeared in Glamour and Parade.

#6780. All About Saoirse Ronan’s Husband, Jack Lowden

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Saoirse Ronan and Jack Lowden have been together for over five years. They tend to keep their lives together very private, but have posted some shots on social media as their relationship progressed.

In July 2024, it was reported that the couple had gotten secretly married. With just a few friends and family members present, they had a ceremony in Edinburgh, Scotland, around the weekend of July 20, 2024. In spring 2025, Ronan revealed her first pregnancy, and the couple was seen pushing a stroller that September.

Here’s everything to know about Lowden and his relationship with Ronan so far.

Lowden is also an actor from Scotland, with a long career onstage. In 2013, he won both an Olivier Award and the Ian Charleson Award for his role as Oswald in Ibsen’s Ghosts. He’s also had some significant roles in film and TV, including Christopher Nolan’s Dunkirk.

In 2020, he bought a home in west Cork with Ronan, but they also live in London and have a terrier named Fran. The couple shares a production company with producer Dominic Norris. Their film The Outrun stars Ronan.

In December 2018, reports surfaced that they were dating after they were seen together at an after-party at Central Park’s Tavern on the Green. The pair played husband and wife in the film Mary Queen of Scots. Sightings of them over the next few years were sparse, and they infrequently appear together on social media. In 2020, they were photographed out in London. In 2022, they were spotted at the Australian Open together. On New Year’s Eve 2023, Lowden shared a mirror selfie of them to celebrate.

In 2019, a reporter for The Telegraph asked Ronan, “Am I allowed to ask if you’re going out with the person that everyone thinks you’re going out with?”

Earlier that year, Lowden was asked about Ronan by ES Magazine and answered the same way, but with more words.

“Oh I’m not going to talk about that. I don’t like talking about that side of life,” he said. “Most of the actors I know live pretty normal lives, it’s just every now and again they have to do something ridiculous or extraordinary. My life hasn’t changed. People seem more interested—I guess that’s changed, for sure.”

Lowden has gushed about Ronan when talking about her acting, including in an interview with i newspaper.

“The woman is a force of nature, on- and offscreen, and utterly fearless and a brilliant leader as well,” he said. “My first love is actors, and when you’re given a Ferrari like her, it’s all about how you make everything good for the Ferrari to show off. Don’t make a Ferrari do what a Renault Clio does. It’s a dreadful analogy, but they’re like gold dust, good actors. When there’s someone like that, you just have to facilitate them and you will get gold. And my God, did we.”

He praised her again in a March 2025 interview with The Times, saying that seeing her perform in Mary Queen of Scots inspired him to keep acting and not quit. “There was me at 27 thinking about stopping because I felt self-conscious or like I didn’t know how to do it,” he said. “And then watching this 23-year-old carry the weight of a huge film like that with ease and grace and majesty. It was remarkable to watch. She makes it look so easy, like breathing. It was totally inspiring.”

The pair made their debut on the carpet as a married couple at the 76th Primetime Emmy Awards at the Peacock Theater in Los Angeles on Sept. 15, 2024. Lowden wore a classic black tux with satin lapels. Ronan was wearing a custom Louis Vuitton two-piece with a sleeveless top and trailing skirt. Around her neck, she wore a stunning diamond necklace by Bulgari.

In an October 2024 interview with USA Today, Ronan shared that she once thought she’d “never” have a partner or a “beautiful network” of people because her career kept her so busy.

“My life has become so much more than just work,” Ronan said. “For a long time, that’s all I did; that’s how I met people and made sense of the world.”

She continued, “I feel like I’m part of a group, which I never thought I would have. I used to think, ‘You’ll never have friends. You’ll never have a partner.’ I was so in my own world, in a lot of ways. So now, to feel like I’ve got a very rich personal and social life—it’s so precious to me.”

In March 2025, Lowden offered rare comments on his relationship with Ronan in his The Times interview. When asked how they keep their marriage strong as two actors whose jobs sometimes require travel, he said, “[We] just have to check in with each other a lot. Obviously it made it a lot easier when we worked on the same project.”

They plan to work together again, he added, “but the question is, how do we do it, because Saoirse, she has ambitions as well to direct.”

On May 22, 2025, Ronan made an appearance at the Louis Vuitton Cruise at Palais des Papes in Avignon, France, wearing a semi-sheer dress by the fashion house that revealed she and Lowden are expecting their first child. The silky black gown was covered in matching black lace trim and folded around her body, showing off her black sandals under the asymmetrical skirt.

On May 31, the couple was seen in London. Ronan wore a form-fitting black bodysuit that appeared to show off her pregnant figure, under a puffer jacket and a sweater tied around her waist. She had on a baseball cap and sunglasses. Lowden was dressed in black sweatpants and an Adidas hoodie and was carrying a backpack.

On Sept. 11, 2025, Hello! reported that the couple had quietly welcomed their first baby. According to the outlet, Ronan and Lowden were seen earlier this month in London pushing a stroller. Lowden later spoke about becoming a father in his November 2026 GQ cover story.

Speaking to People, Ronan revealed that she hopes to act alongside her husband in the future. “I mean, I would love for us to work together again. I’ve said to him I really want him to do a play with me because he’s so incredible onstage, and that’s where he really comes to life,” she said.

“I think that’s where he feels at his best, and it’s not necessarily where I feel at my best, so I would really like to do it with him so he can hold my hand a little bit,” Ronan continued. “I mean, listen, we met through work and we’ve produced stuff together, and we’re very much a part of one another’s work life as well as personal life.”

Still, Ronan said Lowden needs a little more convincing. “It just feels like when the right thing comes along, it would be the obvious choice for us to do it together. But I think he’s more hesitant to work with me than I am with him,” she said. “I don’t know. I think he just finds it weird. He finds it weird working with any of his friends. He gets embarrassed.”

Lowden praised his wife’s acting and opened up about being a father for the first time in his GQ profile.

He reflected on watching her shoot a film in Italy that summer. “I’m going to sound a bit soppy, but getting to watch her act, in the flesh—that’s how we met—is really wonderful,” he said. “She’s so at home doing it.”

He also spoke about welcoming their first child in 2025. “Since I was a kid, I always knew I wanted to be a dad, and now I am,” he admitted. “You become aware that you’ll watch your kid grow up—sounds morbid—and you think, ‘Oh my God, I’m in charge of a kid, and I’m still a kid myself.’”

#6777. Hailey Bieber Is Pledging $5 Million to Women’s Health Research After Her Own Postpartum Struggles

The Rhode founder secretly battled premenstrual dysphoric disorder after giving birth to baby Jack.

Hailey Bieber is putting her money where her mouth is. The Rhode founder is pledging $5 million toward women’s health research inspired by her own tumultuous postpartum journey.

The cause hits close to home for Hailey, who revealed she secretly struggled with premenstrual dysphoric disorder, or PMDD, after giving birth to Jack Blues in August 2024. According to Johns Hopkins Medicine, PMDD is “a much more severe form of premenstrual syndrome,” or PMS.

“After I had my son, I struggled with that a bit, and there’s just not a lot of conversation or research around it,” she told WWD, adding that she’s committed to raising awareness for women’s health issues like PMDD. The condition affects 1.6 percent of women and girls globally, according to research by the University of Oxford.

“Conversation, for me personally, makes me feel less alone,” she continued. “When we can identify with each other and have conversations relating to each other and talking about the things we’re dealing with and struggling with, I do think it makes it feel like less of a lonely journey.”

Hailey also shared she “feels really drawn to women’s health” because she and her loved ones have had different diagnoses that affected their lifestyles.

“I have a sister who struggles with PCOS. I have lots of friends that struggle with it, with endometriosis, with a lot of different, very specific women’s health issues,” she explained. “As a woman, I feel frustrated by the lack of research, the lack of awareness, the lack of certain conversation around it.”

The beauty mogul’s generous pledge will reportedly “support medical research, education, and care in historically underfunded areas, including pregnancy and postpartum health and autoimmune conditions.”

According to WWD, proposals have already been submitted by research institutions, and grant recipients will be announced sometime next year.

#6776. The Kitchen Trends Designers Expect to See Everywhere in 2027

The National Kitchen & Bath Association’s 2027 forecast has arrived.

Above: the kitchen of an Athens home designed by Nashmita Rajiah.

Partial walls, hidden appliances, and pantries with room for prep are among the kitchen features expected to gain ground next year, according to the National Kitchen & Bath Association’s 2027 Kitchen Trends Report. Based on a survey of 830 kitchen-industry professionals—including designers, manufacturers, remodelers, and architects—the report looks at the layouts, materials, lighting, and technology they expect to be popular over the next three years. Here are the main takeaways.

NKBA reports growing interest in partial walls, movable partitions, and separate kitchen zones—a shift from last year’s forecast, when open layouts were still gaining ground. Dedicated prep spaces and back kitchens are also emerging features, cited by nearly half of designers.

Kitchens are still expected to get bigger, with 52 percent of designers anticipating growth. The kitchen’s surrounding rooms are growing, too. NKBA reports that mudrooms and laundry spaces are increasingly being integrated into kitchen plans. The vast majority of designers expect attached mudrooms to be popular over the next three years, with 45 percent anticipating mudrooms getting larger and 43 percent expecting laundry spaces to grow.

Appliances are increasingly being incorporated into cabinetry rather than left on display. Eighty-five percent of designers agree cabinets are being designed to hide appliances and other functional elements, and roughly two-thirds say open shelving is on its way out in favor of enclosed storage.

Pantries are taking on more work, too. Nearly 90 percent agree they are becoming multifunctional extensions of the kitchen, and three-quarters expect dedicated small-appliance stations to be popular. Separate beverage areas continue to gain support, with 90 percent anticipating their popularity, up from 85 percent in the 2026 report.

Neutrals remain the leading color of choice, followed by greens and blues. For cabinetry, light and medium tones draw equal interest, and white oak is the preferred wood for roughly half of designers. Islands don’t necessarily have to match: a little more than half expect them to feature a different color from the surrounding cabinets. Quartzite and quartz lead countertop preferences, with nearly half of designers favoring honed or matte finishes.

The lighting forecast includes under-cabinet lights, illuminated cabinet interiors, nighttime path lighting, sconces, and pendants. Eighty-four percent of designers agree kitchens will incorporate multiple layers of light, and 78 percent say lighting is increasingly being used as a design feature rather than just a functional necessity. Under-cabinet lighting leads the forecast at 87 percent, followed by interior-cabinet lighting at 76 percent.

NKBA identifies ventilation, water filtration, touchless faucets, and sound-absorbing materials among the report’s wellness features. Designers are also discussing ways to make kitchens more resilient: 65 percent cite leak-detection systems and automatic shut-off valves, 62 percent cite backup power, and a little over half point to ventilation and air filtration.

Julia Cancilla is the social media & news editor at ELLE Decor, where she oversees the brand's socials and covers design, pop culture, and emerging trends. She also authors the monthly ELLE Decoroscope column. Her work has appeared in Inked magazine, House Beautiful, Marie Claire, and more.

#6775. Nathan Fielder’s Elizabeth Holmes Documentary, You Can See Everything: What We Know So Far

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Elizabeth Holmes’s story is getting told in a new project with Nathan Fielder at the helm. The comedian has released his new documentary titled You Can See Everything about Holmes, founder of the now-defunct biotech company Theranos, before she began serving her 11-year prison sentence for committing fraud.

Unlike a typical documentary, the film features Fielder chatting with Holmes in a Q&A format, while an unnamed A-list actress reenacts the events of the documentary in asides as Holmes. Here’s everything we know about You Can See Everything.

The official logline for the documentary reads: “34 days before she’s sent to prison, notorious Silicon Valley founder Elizabeth Holmes invites a skeptical film crew to document her every move. What begins as an intimate portrait of an enigmatic felon becomes a mind-bending three-year journey into the abyss. From boundary-pushing directors Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim comes You Can See Everything.”

Audiences were buzzing at Telluride Film Festival, where the doc premiered with a surprise screening on September 6. Variety called the doc “a shameless, fascinating, reality-TV-eats-its-tail look at entitled duplicity” and said “the sensation of ‘WTF? I’ve got to watch this!’ did not subside as the movie went on. It only intensified.”

Deadline reported that “the nearly three-hour-long film drew continuous laughter, frequently triggered by hilarious reaction shots of Fielder...attempting to process what Holmes and Evans [Holmes’s partner] are pushing.” And the L.A. Times said the movie contains “mind-bending twists that meld reality and performance.”

The documentary is currently available to watch in theaters, but it will likely hit HBO Max when it comes to streaming. Warner Bros. Discovery owns HBO and HBO Max, and the company has an agreement that states A24 titles will automatically go to HBO Max as their first stop on streaming after the theatrical window and PVOD (Premium Video on Demand) release windows are over. You Can See Everything currently has no streaming release date.

In addition to Fielder and Holmes, The Hollywood Reporter confirmed that an unnamed “A-list actress” has a role in the making of the documentary. Her identity has not been revealed, as “the filmmakers and fest [Telluride Film Festival] requested that her identity be kept secret until more people have a chance to see the movie.”

As for what viewers can expect from the A-list actress, THR reports her cameo is “the craziest twist of a crazy film,” but her involvement is accidental. After agreeing to meet with Fielder, she finds herself wanting to be involved in the filming process and “winds up becoming a full-fledged collaborator in the film, appearing throughout the rest of it alternatingly as herself, giving commentary on the insanity of the situation, and then in reenactments—long Fielder’s go-to way of making sense of things—as Holmes.”

The first official trailer for the documentary arrived on September 8, featuring Fielder and Holmes having an unsettling conversation.

“I don’t have anything to deceive you on. Of course I’m not deceiving you. I’m engaging with you as a human being. Why would I deceive you? There’s no...there’s no reason for me to do that,” Holmes tells Fielder.

“You’re being real right now?” he asks her, to which Holmes replies, “I’m always being real.”

A first-look image was also released with the trailer, which shows Holmes preparing to give an item to a child, while Fielder looks at her from the background.

The first official trailer arrived on September 23 and provides a close look into some of the conversations that Fielder and Holmes had with each other throughout the film.

“You really feel you did nothing wrong?” Fielder asks her, to which Holmes replies, “I did not.”

He presses her and follows up the question with, “So where did things get lost? How are you where you are right now?”

Holmes finds herself at a loss: “I wish I knew. I don’t know. I don’t feel like I understand that,” she tells him.

At another point in the trailer, Fielder questions how Holmes could be so calm about his filming her every move. When he asks Holmes if she has any “worry” about him being at her home 24/7, she answers, “I don’t think so. I think this is the moment in time to capture this story before I’m gone.”

The comedian also manages to interview Holmes’s partner, William “Billy” Evans, who reveals he fell in love with her in their first conversation. “Weren’t you worried she was scamming you?” Fielder asks.

Evans coolly responds, “100 percent, but what does she has to gain from me?”

When Fielder reminds him that his family is wealthy—Evans is the heir to the Evans Hotel Group—he responds, “I think there’s a lot of different levels of wealth.”

Neither Holmes nor Evans have seen the doc, Oppenheim revealed during an October Q&A after the film’s Los Angeles screening. “Neither of them have seen the film. Elizabeth is still incarcerated; Billy hasn’t seen it,” the director said, with The Hollywood Reporter adding that Evans has “a strained relationship with the crew.”

Fielder added that Evans “started trying to exert a level of control over the process that made it very hard to engage with him, and it was uncomfortable for us and also A24 and everyone. And I think we sort of had to stop—I think communication broke down basically, and we’ve been in that like state for a little bit. So it’s been challenging in that way.”

On November 2, Evans revealed the “one misconception” about his relationship with Holmes following the release of the movie. “That I somehow control Liz. Anyone who actually knows her would find that idea hilarious,” he responded to an X user.

Evans added, “She’s fiercely independent, strong willed, and my best friend. We are partners in every sense. We challenge each other, disagree, and face the world together. Her closest friends are also some of my closest friends. If I ever mistreated her, they’d have my head.”

Yes. In 2019, HBO released The Inventor: Out for Blood in Silicon Valley, which explored the rise and fall of Holmes and her multibillion-dollar company, Theranos.

In 2022, The Dropout—also about the rise and fall of Holmes and Theranos—premiered on Hulu. Amanda Seyfried starred as Holmes in the miniseries.

#6771. House Party: Angelica Hicks

With a new Paperless Post collection, the illustrator shares her rules for a party worth RSVPing yes to.

Welcome to House Party, where we ask expert hosts for their top entertaining tips, tricks, and tabletop tactics. Come party with us.

Angelica Hicks has a talent for making high fashion out of everyday materials. The British artist, illustrator, and fashion satirist is known for using household supplies to recreate runway and red-carpet looks, capturing couture’s most elaborate details with candy wrappers and duct tape. A sharp eye runs in the family: Her father is the artist and interior designer Ashley Hicks, and her grandfather was the influential decorator David Hicks. But her visual wit is distinctly her own, evident in everything from her illustrative book Tongue in Chic to her collaborations with Gucci.

Her newly launched invitation collection for Paperless Post brings that humor to entertaining, with designs including a shrimp holding a martini. Hicks’s own hosting philosophy is similarly spirited, though she’s quite serious about a well-considered seating chart. For House Party, we asked her about the best parties she’s attended, the merits of an emergency ice delivery, and the extravagance she’d happily import from Japan.

ELLE Decor: What are your three essentials when hosting?

Angelica Hicks: A great invite that sets the tone before the event. A shrimp holding a martini inviting you to a cocktail party perhaps?

What’s your top entertaining/hosting tip?

People love to complain about placement, but I am pro placement. I think telling people where to sit, and actually spending time considering it, will only benefit the flow of conversation—especially when mixing people who might not already know each other. I think it’s a fabulous thing to do when hosting a dinner party.

Who, dead or alive, is coming to your dream dinner party?

Noël Coward, Joan Crawford, Aretha Franklin, Cristóbal Balenciaga.

Announcing dietary restrictions upon arrival.

If you could add one exorbitantly extravagant component to a party, what would it be?

I think I would fly in urchin from Japan if it were the season.

Who threw the best party you’ve ever attended?

Unemployed Magazine always throws an excellent Halloween party. What makes it so great is that they select one iconic person as a theme and everyone dresses up as that person or someone in the universe. Last year was Liz Taylor, and the year before was Princess Diana.

I'm ashamed to say I rarely bring anything. But once I have arrived, if I notice there is something missing, I will order it, which I think is far more helpful. A bag of ice can come in much more handy than a mid bottle of red.

What historic party would you have liked to have attended?

I think I would have had great fun dressing up for the Rothschild Surrealist Ball.

Buy too much wine; you'll probably go through it.

I don’t have a dishwasher, so I always lament the washing up.

#6770. Safe Word: The Best Jewelry Launches of November 2026

Safe Word is a series highlighting the latest trends in the jewelry market, keeping you up to date on all things that sparkle and shine.

November’s jewelry launches are looking both backward and forward, with designers revisiting their roots, reworking archival ideas, and stepping into entirely new territory. First up, Pomellato reaches back to 1997 to revive “Moneta Orientale” as a colorful modern talisman; David Yurman celebrates the magic of a New York holiday with Michael B. Jordan, Yerin Ha, and Inde Navarrette; Vhernier revisits its iconic “Trottola” design with new proportions and contrasting materials; Di Volpe reimagines the classic tennis bracelet with customizable gemstones in “DiSegno”; and Brent Neale transforms the classic bow into sculptural fine jewelry with “Wrap It Up.”

Elsewhere, Grace Winfrey looks to Italian good-luck charms for the colorful “Poppy Fortuna”; Robinson Pelham checks into “Suite 96” to celebrate 30 years of bold jewelry; Le Vian brings Georgian-era romance to Jared with a new collection of warm-toned diamonds; and Wempe puts traditional goldsmithing at the center of “au:thentic,” inspired by the humble jump ring. From archival revivals and meaningful motifs to innovative designs and new creative directions, November’s launches prove that sometimes the best way forward starts with looking back.

For the rest of the month, we’ve got you covered on all the jewelry-related happenings worth knowing about.

Pomellato is looking to its archives for its latest good-luck charm. For the newest chapter of “Memorie d’Archivio,” the maison revives Moneta Orientale, a 1997 pendant inspired by the ancient symbolism of a circle surrounding a square, representing the harmony between heaven and earth. Reimagined for today in 33 stone-and-pavé combinations, the design pairs colorful hard stones including black jade, amazonite, sodalite, rhodochrosite, and labradorite with Pomellato’s signature irregular pavé of diamonds, sapphires, rubies, and more. Offered in two proportions, each pendant transforms the archival motif into a modern talisman for prosperity and protection.

David Yurman Captures the Magic of a New York Holiday

David Yurman is celebrating the magic of the holidays in New York City with a new campaign featuring global brand ambassador Michael B. Jordan alongside actresses Yerin Ha and Inde Navarrette. Set against a snow-covered Washington Square Park, the campaign captures a series of quintessential New York moments, from Jordan and Ha facing off in a spirited card game to Navarrette hailing a cab in search of the perfect holiday gift. Throughout the campaign, the cast wears some of the house’s most recognizable designs, including 18-karat gold Cable bracelets and pieces from the “Starburst” and “Armory” collections. Directed by Bradford Young and photographed by Drew Vickers, the campaign brings together the spirit of the city, the joy of togetherness, and the timeless appeal of David Yurman’s jewelry.

Vhernier’s ‘Trottola’ Takes on a New Form

Vhernier is revisiting one of its most recognizable designs with a new interpretation of “Trottola,” the sculptural collection originally introduced in 2003. Inspired by the continuous motion of a spinning top, the latest designs play with proportions, contrasting materials, and asymmetrical shapes across rings, bracelets, necklaces, and earrings. Polished rose gold meets glossy black jet, with select pieces featuring diamond pavé for an added contrast of light and dark. The collection also focuses on versatility, from rings designed for stacking to convertible earclips that can be worn as studs or dangling earrings. With its fluid forms and unexpected material combinations, “Trottola” brings a fresh perspective to Vhernier’s signature sculptural approach to jewelry.

Di Volpe is rethinking the classic tennis bracelet with “DiSegno,” a new customizable fine jewelry system designed to change with its wearer. Using concealed, patent-pending connections, individual cultured diamonds and gemstones, called Elements, can be removed, rearranged, or added by hand, allowing wearers to transform bracelets into necklaces or earrings without compromising the seamless look of traditional tennis jewelry. The collection launches with 28 stone-and-cut combinations, including rubies, emeralds, sapphires, and diamonds, alongside a limited-edition collaboration with Lebanese artist Ali Cha’aban featuring five sculptural designs. With new cuts, colors, and artist collaborations planned for future releases, “DiSegno” makes the case for fine jewelry that’s never quite finished.

Brent Neale Reimagines the Bow in Fine Jewelry

Brent Neale is putting a bow on the holiday season with “Wrap It Up,” a new fine jewelry collection that transforms the familiar ribbon motif into sculptural works of art. Drawing inspiration from Old Hollywood glamour and the femininity of the 1960s, the designer plays with the contrast between rigid precious metals and the soft movement of fabric, crafting bows that twist, curl, and unfurl in 18-karat gold and platinum. Colored gemstones sit nestled within the ribbon-like forms, while pavé diamonds accentuate every curve and fold. Equal parts playful and precious, the collection brings a little gift-wrapped glamour to the jewelry box.

Grace Winfrey’s Latest Collection Is All About Good Fortune

Grace Winfrey is bringing a little extra luck to the jewelry box with “Poppy Fortuna,” a new fine jewelry collection inspired by the natural beauty of the Italian landscape and symbols of good fortune. Scarlet poppies, sunflowers, bees, four-leaf clovers, horseshoes, and ladybugs come to life through vibrant gemstones and intricate pavé, creating miniature mosaics of color and texture. Highlights include a Lucky Bangle combining three good-luck charms, sculptural sunflower earrings with tiny ladybugs tucked into their petals, and layered charm necklaces designed to feel like modern keepsakes. Handcrafted in New York City, each piece transforms familiar symbols of prosperity, protection, and happiness into playful, colorful works of art.

Robinson Pelham Channels ’90s Glamour for Its Anniversary

Robinson Pelham is closing out its 30th anniversary celebrations with “Suite 96,” a new campaign and collection launch inspired by the glamour of late-’90s London. Set inside a private suite at St. James’s Hotel & Club in Mayfair, the campaign reimagines jewelry as the ultimate room service, with diamonds arriving on trays, gold spilling out of wardrobes, and plenty of pieces to layer before a night out. At the center is “Studio 30,” a four-piece anniversary collection featuring diamond necklaces and statement earrings, including the sculptural Via and Hira necklaces, cascading diamond drops, and the bold Solstice Supreme. Alongside the anniversary designs, the house introduces new collections and additions to existing favorites, from chunky gold links and graphic heart rings to colorful enamel and gemstone combinations. Together, the launches celebrate three decades of Robinson Pelham’s bold, playful approach to fine jewelry.

Le Vian and Jared Revive Georgian-Era Romance

Le Vian is bringing a little old-world romance to Jared Jewelers with its new exclusive “Georgian Chocolate & Nude Diamonds” collection. Inspired by the candlelit jewels of the Georgian era, when jewelry was designed to capture and reflect the warmth of natural light, the collection reimagines historic buttercup settings in polished Honey Gold, with eight rounded prongs framing each natural diamond. The designs pair Le Vian’s signature Chocolate and Nude Diamonds with warm gold detailing across solitaire rings, anniversary bands, tennis bracelets, necklaces, and earrings. Select styles introduce colorful gemstones including Chocolate Quartz, Green Apple Peridot, and Grape Amethyst, giving the vintage-inspired silhouettes a contemporary update. With its sculptural settings and warm-toned stones, the collection brings the romance of antique jewelry into the present.

Wempe’s ‘Au:thentic’ Celebrates the Art of Goldsmithing

Wempe’s new gender-inclusive fine jewelry collection, “Au:thentic,” puts craftsmanship front and center, finding inspiration in the foundational skills taught to aspiring goldsmiths. The collection takes its shape from the jump ring, one of the first techniques Patrick Sieroslawski learned as an apprentice more than 22 years ago, translating its form to necklaces, bracelets, rings, earrings, and pendants. Handcrafted at Wempe’s atelier in Germany, the pieces come in 18-karat yellow, white, and rose gold, with select styles set with diamonds. “Au:thentic” also marks the beginning of a multiyear rollout, with three additional chapters set to expand on its design language through twists, wraps, and interlocking links.

#6769. Who Pays for ‘Love Is Blind’ Weddings? Let’s Break Down the Cost (and How Much $ Netflix Actually Drops)

It’s that time of the year again! After partaking in the world’s most bizarre version of speed dating possible, the couples of Love Is Blind are ready to get married on camera. The weddings are a chance for everyone to either say “I do” or “I don’t” in front of their friends, family, and thousands of strangers logged into their friend’s ex-boyfriend’s Netflix subscription, and you might be wondering who foots the bill.

Thankfully, the show’s couples do not pay for their own wedding ceremony. Netflix takes care of the cost, and yep, there is a strict budget.

Wedding planner Slomique Hawrylo told Tudum that she has couples fill out a questionnaire about their wedding theme (“traditional” or “boho” for example), their likes and dislikes, and a general vibe. “What I’m doing is looking at what they write on a piece of paper, and getting the feelings and emotions from that,” she explained. “It’s capturing their style, what they want, a certain color.”

And considering there can be multiple a week depending on how many couples are getting married (or at least making it to their ceremony) per season, it’s a lot of work. The team is tasked with hiring local vendors, figuring out food, florals, the cake—the typical things that would go into planning any non-televised wedding (only on a way tighter time crunch).

The show allots a pool of money to plan five weddings on the show, and it’s up to Slomique how that money is allotted. In season 4, Tudum reports that florals cost $50,000 to $60,000, while tents were $120,000.

Any more would stretch the budget too much!

In some cases, the bride and groom will pay for extras that the show is unwilling to cover. Examples? Specific photographers that are outside the budget, specific nail artists, or details like monograms, which are “really, really difficult” for the team in the short time frame.

Love Is Blind creator Chris Coelen told Newsweek, “If there’s anything over and above what we would normally do, then [the couples] are most certainly welcome to do that.”

There was one “I do” on Love Is Blind season 11, and the show clearly went all out! Though...did the relationship last? Spoilers below:

#6767. Costco's New Porch Goose Feels So '80s Coded, and Fans Are Running to Stores to Find It

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Think of these backyard birds as your new mascot: They can sit on your porch, front yard, or terrace to offer cheerful charm for visitors and passersby. Though porch geese were once popular in the 1980s, they’re making a comeback in a big way—and are even available at Costco for $35. If you ask us, it’s a small price for so much joy.

When most people think about buying home products from Costco, they usually think of strictly practical purchases: Heaps of toilet paper rolls, a shed to conceal patio furniture, or even furniture that can turn a home office into a bedroom on demand. But make no mistake, even Costco is brimming with whimsy.

Clocking in at 24 inches tall, this porch goose from Evergreen Enterprises is the Goldilocks of outdoor decor: It’s large enough to make a statement but not as cumbersome as, say, a 12-foot-tall skeleton. The metal frame is sturdy enough to hold its own should a gust of wind sweep your neighborhood, while the powder-coated finish keeps rust and corrosion to a minimum. That said, if your goose gets a little dirty, you can disconnect the body from its webbed feet and give it a quick wipe-down.

You have to admit this porch goose is adorable on its own, but what makes this pick even cuter is that it comes with a pair of outfits: Shoppers can choose between a gardener ensemble and rain slicker or a “grill master” look, as well as a tropical shirt and lei. But, honestly, why stop there? A quick Amazon search yields clothes for Thanksgiving, Christmas, and even Valentine’s Day.

Sure, adding a few more costumes to your goose’s closet is an extra expense, but if you want to bring some smile-inducing quirk, it’s an investment that’s so worth it.

#6766. On the Couch with Christopher John Rogers

In honor of his new collaboration with The Rug Company, the fashion designer shares his inspirations and style icons.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE Decor editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Christopher John Rogers has an eye for color. The fashion designer, born in Baton Rouge and based in New York, established his self-titled label in 2016. Over the past decade, he’s become known for his use of bold tones and voluminous silhouettes, and for dressing the likes of Michelle Obama and Cardi B. Now, Rogers is taking his style to the floor in a new collaboration with The Rug Company.

The collection, which Rogers designed with Will Cooper, creative director of The Rug Company, feels alive, with vibrant, swooshing marks, multicolored doodles in wool and silk, and wavy threads that appear to dance across the floor. “The scribbles, the brushstrokes, the happy accidents, the lines that don't quite meet,” Rogers tells ELLE Decor. “I love the idea of taking something spontaneous and seemingly incidental and rendering it permanent through this incredibly meticulous, centuries-old craft.”

Launching tomorrow, the collaboration includes six styles across ten colorways. But Rogers has more planned beyond womenswear, “exploring new ways for the brand to exist beyond the wardrobe,” he says. “I'm particularly excited by opportunities that allow us to think about color, proportion, texture, and materiality through different disciplines, whether that's furniture, interiors, or something entirely unexpected.”

Below, Rogers takes ELLE Decor’s On the Couch survey, sharing a few of his favorite pieces, destinations, and entertaining takes.

Favorite Sofa: Pierre Paulin's ABCD sofa for Artifort. The color theory is delicious, and I'd happily sacrifice a little comfort for something that fabulous.

Architect You Admire: Ettore Sottsass. I love the clarity of his vision and his ability to make the simplest shapes feel monumental.

Destination Museum: The Louisiana Museum of Modern Art in Denmark

Go-to Galleries: 52 Walker, Broadway Gallery, and Salon 94 in New York.

Favorite Artist/Artwork: I've recently fallen for Domenica Brockman. We have one of her paintings in the studio, and I'm constantly struck by how much emotion she can communicate through deceptively simple arrangements of color.

Vintage Piece of Your Dreams: An original Verner Panton Living Tower.

Hotels to Check Into: Hotel Saint Vincent in New Orleans, and La Mamounia in Marrakech.

Entertaining Tips: Great lighting, a boozy cocktail, and a playlist with a point of view. Don't overthink it!

Style Icons: Bert and Ernie, and Isaac Mizrahi.

Required Reading: Interaction of Color by Josef Albers and The Bluest Eye by Toni Morrison. Two very different but equally essential ways of learning to see.

Cocktail or Mocktail of Choice: It's a toss-up between a wet Roku martini and a Cynar spritz.

Favorite Restaurant: Cervo’s in the Lower East Side, Rolo's in Ridgewood, Frenchette in Tribeca, and Santa Panza in Bushwick.

Favorite Jeweler: Presley Oldham and Bernard James for contemporary jewelry, and vintage Alexander Calder or Alber Elbaz-era Lanvin for something a little more fantastical.

Annie Goldsmith is the senior editor and digital lead at ELLE Decor, where she covers design, culture, style, and trends. She previously held positions at The Information, covering technology and culture, and Town & Country, writing about news, entertainment, and fashion. Her work has also appeared in Vogue, Rolling Stone, and the SF Standard.
